## Snapshot
Micron is one of three scaled DRAM makers (with Samsung and SK Hynix) and a top-tier NAND supplier, selling DRAM/NAND/NOR under the Micron and Crucial brands (FY25 10-K, 2025-10-03). DRAM is ~79% of revenue, NAND ~20-21% (Q2 FY26, 2026-03-18). The franchise is reorganized around four data-center-centric BUs: Cloud Memory (CMBU), Core Data Center (CDBU), Mobile & Client (MCBU), and Auto & Embedded (AEBU). The thesis hinges on memory being repositioned "from a system component to a strategic asset" for AI — Micron supplies the full memory hierarchy that agentic inference consumes: HBM next to the accelerator, LPDDR/DDR5/SoCAM expansion memory on the CPU, and high-capacity QLC/TLC data-center SSDs for the growing KV-cache/context tier (Sathya Kumar via UBS Arcuri call, 2026-05-15). It is the #2-3 HBM player behind SK Hynix; only credible third source on HBM4 given Samsung's product issues.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) DRAM (~79% rev, incl. HBM) · 2) HBM (margin-accretive AI memory, share≈DRAM share) · 3) NAND/eSSD (~20-21% rev) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscalers + AI accelerator OEMs (NVIDIA/AMD) for HBM; mobile/client + auto/embedded OEMs; supply-constrained — fills only 50-66% of key-customer demand, CY26 HBM sold out |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC (HBM4 base die, N12/N3) + IP partners for leading-edge logic; ASML EUV tools (1γ DRAM); cleanroom/fab construction |

## Current state (latest quarter)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-15 — UBS TRIMS NEAR-TERM ESTIMATES, AND THE REASON IS NAND, NOT DRAM. Buy and the $1,625 PT stand; the cut is scoped to a wider range of possible NAND pricing outcomes through year-end and into C1H27.** (UBS · **Timothy Arcuri**, Buy, PT **US$1,625**, relayed by UBS Sector Sales · Robert Ruple, 2026-08-15. ⚠️ **Sales relay of a UBS Research note that was NOT read — the direction and the reason are captured, the MAGNITUDE of the trim and the revised absolute estimates are NOT. Pull the primary before modelling.**)
- **As relayed, verbatim:** *"Amid a bit of **NAND noise** with **DRAM even [better] than our expectations** Tim Arcuri is making **slight adjustments to our model** following our latest round of industry checks, the net of which is that we **slightly trim estimates in the near-term due to a WIDER RANGE OF POTENTIAL PRICING OUTCOMES FOR NAND through year-end and into C1H27**."*
- 🔴 **THE SHAPE IS THE POINT AND IT IS TWO-SIDED IN A WAY THE HEADLINE HIDES: DRAM is running BETTER than UBS expected while NAND uncertainty widens. For MU specifically that is a favourable mix — this page's whole thesis rests on DRAM/HBM, not NAND — so a trim driven by NAND variance is a smaller negative for MU than the same trim would be for [[SNDK]] or [[KIOXIA]].** ➜ **Cross-check against the independent NAND spot evidence the same week: Citrini reports TLC NAND spot RISING since mid-July (first time since the March peak) while QLC spot is FLAT (2026-08-14). A widening range, not a break.**
- **Sentiment context from the same desk, and it is the cleanest statement of the memory debate on the wiki right now:** *"**Memory remains the most controversial.** I sense that **hedge funds continue to lean tactically SHORT**, with debates centered around whether **LTAs have capped upside, NAND fundamentals are weakening, and capital returns are appropriate despite the industry's long-term cyclicality**. **Yet market action tells a different story.** Samsung (+16.5% 5D), SK Hynix (+13.7% 5D), **Micron (+10.5% 5D)**, and SanDisk (+35% 5D) all rallied sharply."* **MU is on UBS's semis LONG-BIASED positioning list.**
- **The corroborating positioning read from MS, arrived at independently:** memory **de-grossed hardest in the late-June/late-July unwind and KEPT reducing even as other AI sectors re-grossed**, leaving *"positioning… CLEANER in Memory for the next leg"*; **Shawn Kim's framing is "3x NTM P/E suggests that markets UNDERESTIMATE THE DURATION of Memory tightness"** while still conceding *"classic 'late-cycle' characteristics… price increases can't hold 7x historical peak forever."* **And the demand-side anchor: "NVDA CEO reiterated MULTI-YEAR MEMORY SHORTAGE during the Morgan Stanley NDR."** (MS Sales · Skyler Risom, "Friday Five," 2026-08-14 — *not* MS Research.)
- **DATED CATALYST — MANAGEMENT IS ON THE ROAD AND THE AGENDA IS THE DEBATE:** **UBS-arranged Micron CFO/IR marketing in Singapore, 2026-08-17**, with **Arcuri travelling in Asia with MU management next week**, where *"**LTAs, pricing, capital allocation and the durability [of the] memory cycle will remain front and center**."* ➜ **Every one of those four items is an open question on this page. Watch for a UBS callback note in the following days — the 07-23 [[TXN]] callback precedent on [[ADI]] shows these produce first-hand company language ahead of the print.**
- **Sector context, same week:** **SIA June data — global semiconductor sales a record $147.8bn, +10.8% m/m, 310bps above the 10-yr and 370bps above the 5-yr seasonal average, described by UBS as a "Memory-Led Surge"; UBS's preferred US names are MU and [[NVDA]]** (UBS · Arcuri, via Ruple 08-15). **APAC drawdown context: MSCI ex-Japan IT −20% since 22 June, SOX −16%, with [[SKHYNIX]] −51% from peak and [[SAMSUNG]] −37%; UBS's Nicolas Gaudois recommends revisiting AI-exposed sub-sectors in the order Memory > MLCCs > Semicap.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-12 — JUNE WSTS IS THE TENTH CONSECUTIVE MONTH OF ACCELERATION AND A NEW RECORD: MEMORY REVENUE +369% Y/Y ON ASPs +225% Y/Y. AND JPM'S FORWARD CURVE IS THE USEFUL PART — DRAM ASP +29% IN 2027 AND +7% IN 2028 AFTER A +235% 2026.** (JPM Asia Pacific / Gokul Hariharan, "June WSTS: YoY growth accelerates to new record on surging memory ASPs and logic price hikes", 2026-08-12.)

- **The June data:** total semi revenue **+134% y/y** (vs +128% in May), **logic +38%** (vs +34%), **memory +369%** (vs +353%). **Tenth consecutive month of acceleration; a new record for y/y growth.** Drivers per JPM: **sustained AI compute demand (TSMC visibility now extending into 2029-30)**, **memory ASP strength on structurally tight supply/demand**, and **strengthening mature-foundry pricing power on surging AI-peripheral demand**.
- **🔴 THE PRICE/VOLUME DECOMPOSITION, which is what this page needs and the headline hides: total semi ASPs +117% y/y, memory ASPs +225% y/y — while memory UNITS were only +44% y/y (vs +41% in May).** ➜ **The memory cycle is ~5x more price than volume. That is the definition of a cycle that mean-reverts on ASP rather than on units, and it is the single most important frame for underwriting 2027–28 memory earnings.**
- **🔴 THE FORWARD CURVE — JPM's numbers, and they are a decelerating-but-still-positive path, not a crash: "We see DRAM ASP rising 29%/7% in 2027/28E after a 235% rally in 2026E, as flexible pricing structures allow memory makers to leverage the current shortage market."** ⚠️ **Read that carefully against any model on this page that straight-lines 2026 pricing: JPM has the rally continuing, but the second derivative turns hard negative in 2027 and the 2028 increment is nearly nil.**
- **🔴 LTA STRUCTURE — the terms are disclosed, and they are extraordinarily supplier-favourable: "LTAs in 2Q26 showed favorable terms for memory makers, with PREPAYMENT MIX (20-25% OF LTA VALUE) and LARGE VOLUME CONTRIBUTION (50-70%) indicating strong supplier bargaining power. The MAJORITY OF LTAs ARE CENTERED AROUND AI/SERVER COMPUTE, CARRYING A 30-40% PRICE PREMIUM OVER THE REST."** ➜ **Three separately usable facts: customers are pre-paying a fifth to a quarter of contract value; half to 70% of volume is locked; and the AI/server tier prices 30–40% above the rest of the book. The last one is the mix lever that carries blended ASP even if spot rolls over.**
- **🔴 CAPACITY AND HBM MIX — the supply-side answer, and it is dated: "we expect DRAM wafer allocation to HBM to continue to increase, reaching 32% CAPACITY MIX BY 2028E-END… We forecast DRAM WSPM to reach 2.85M by 2028 end from 1.9M at 2025 end, STILL BELOW THE EXPECTED DEMAND due to 2-2.5 YEARS OF LEAD TIME FROM GROUNDBREAKING TO PRODUCTION RAMP."** ⚠️ **+50% DRAM wafer capacity over three years, with a third of it going to HBM, and JPM still calls it short. The 2–2.5yr groundbreaking-to-ramp lead time is why: capacity decisions taken today do not relieve 2027.**
- **🔴 THE NET-NEW DEMAND VECTOR, and JPM flags it as under-appreciated: "Memory demand BROADENING OUT FROM GPU TO CPU appears to have been UNDERESTIMATED BY INVESTORS and remains a KEY SOURCE OF POTENTIAL UPWARD REVISIONS," with AI CPU unit TAM expected to grow at a 155% CAGR.** ➜ **Cross-read: this is the same mechanism [[LRCX]]'s CFO cited on the same day for raising WFE-per-AI-capex from ~$8bn to $9–10bn per $100bn ("inference becomes increasingly dominant and pulls through not only GPU/XPU compute, but also greater memory and CPU intensity"). Two independent sources, same week, same causal claim — that is a corroborated structural datapoint, not a single-house view.** Cross-ref [[LRCX]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[INTC]].
- **⚠️ THE DEMAND-DESTRUCTION COUNTERWEIGHT, from the same day and worth carrying on this page rather than only on the buyers': UBS expects "memory price inflation and mix allocation towards higher-end products will likely constrain demand for low-to-mid tier PCs, while pass-through of some costs will also pressure unit volume"** (UBS / Timothy Arcuri, CPU Industry Update, 2026-08-12). ➜ **The first named channel through which memory pricing starts eating its own end-market. Watch PC units.**
- **Logic/foundry context in the same note (relevant as the competing claim on the same wafer/equipment pool): logic +38% y/y, 34 consecutive months of growth; JPM raises [[TSM]] capex to $62/$81/$90bn FY26/27/28 (from $58/$78/$84bn), with N5-and-below capacity growing at a 24% CAGR 2026-28; mature-foundry price hikes accelerating (PSMC +10–15% on both 8" and 12" in 1H; VIS +5–15% on 8"), with a further round expected in 2H26 and 2027.**

**🆕 2026-08-10 — UBS marks HBM UP and NAND DOWN, PT unchanged at $1,625 (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-08-10).** Post-checks model tweak: **C2026/27/28E EPS to $74.13 / $184.89 / $265.65** (all "well above consensus"), EPS held **>$160 out in C2029** and **>$450B cumulative FCF through C2028** — Arcuri's framing is "a structural reset in earnings power… should command a shift toward a broader semi multiple." PT **$1,625 unchanged**, now built on **~11x NTM P/E** (MU's 3-yr average, *cut from ~15x prior*) on C2029E EPS ~$165 discounted back to C2028 at ~12% CoE (implied C2028 value ~$148). Buy reiterated.
- **HBM tighter than they thought:** industry blended **HBM ASP +79% Y/Y in C2027 (vs +67% prior)**, HBM4E likely **>$30/GB**; MU-specific **HBM ASP +72% Y/Y (vs +61% prior)** on broadly unchanged MU HBM bits of **~11.65B Gb C2027**. Suppliers are **re-widening the HBM/DDR premium** (UBS has flagged this since April).
- **The VR300 de-spec is a volume offset, not a demand cut:** UBS checks put the first VR300 variant at **384GB HBM4/GPU** (8-Hi × 24Gb × 8 × 2 dies) and a later version at **512GB HBM4E/GPU** (8-Hi × 32Gb × 8 × 2) around **C3Q27** — *below* UBS's prior 768GB — but more VR300 units in the chain lift **C2027 HBM consumption from 58.7B Gb to 61.5B Gb**. (Reconciles with the ~384GB Rubin figure already logged on this page from Fubon 08-03.)
- **DDR unchanged:** industry C3Q contract (incl. LTAs) **+20% Q/Q**; MU DDR ASPs **+20% Q/Q C3Q, +11% Q/Q C4Q**. CXMT bit-supply share modelled **~7% C2025 → ~9% C2027**; UBS calls investor CXMT fear "a bit overstated" on installation/yield/technology gaps. **DRAM undersupplied through at least C2Q28.**
- **NAND is the trim:** C3Q industry contract pricing **+20% Q/Q (vs +28% prior)**, MU **+23% Q/Q (vs +35% prior)**; but bit-demand raised to **+23%/+26% Y/Y C2026/27** and industry NAND revenue to **~$300B C2026 / ~$495B C2027**, with pricing still peaking **C4Q27** because **YMTC is steering ~30k of ~45k wpm of annual additions to DRAM rather than NAND**.

**Going into the print — Barron's earnings preview (Review & Preview, 2026-06-22):** consensus **$20.45 EPS / $35.4B revenue** for the print due Wed 2026-06-24 after close; "shares have surged roughly 750% in the past 12 months amid a shortage of memory chips driven by AI" (FactSet via Barron's, 2026-06-22). The same Barron's issue's "Top CEOs of 2026" profile of CEO Sanjay Mehrotra (Jack Hough) flagged **"Micron is expected to post a record $69 billion in net income in fiscal 2026... shares surging about 1,150% since the start of 2025. Micron stock now trades at 6.7 times projected 12-month sales, up from a historical average closer to three times"** (Barron's, 2026-06-22) — actuals below beat the $20.45/$35.4B consensus decisively.

**F3Q26 (reported 2026-06-24 AH) — a massive beat-and-raise.** Revenue **$41.5B** (+346% y/y, +74% q/q) vs Street ~$34.7B and JPM $35.6B; non-GAAP gross margin **84.9%** (all-time high, +350bps vs Street 81.4%); DRAM ASPs +63% QoQ, NAND ASPs +87% QoQ (JPMe). Stock **+19% AH to a new ATH** (JPM / Harlan Sur; BofA / Vivek Arya; Barclays / Tom O'Malley; MS / Joseph Moore; UBS / Tim Arcuri; Citi / Atif Malik, 2026-06-25). CEO Sanjay Mehrotra: "We don't really see when the supply is going to be able to meet demand" (MU F3Q26 call, 2026-06-24).

**Guidance — F4Q26 (August):** revenue **$50B** (+18-23% QoQ per Fubon), GM ~**86%**, EPS **$31** — far above prior Street consensus $24.1 and the buyside bogey of ~$28 (Fubon/Jefferies / Richard Hsia; JPM / Harlan Sur, 2026-06-25).

**Terafab read-through — memory explicitly in scope (UBS "SpaceX's Terafab", 2026-07-07):** Elon Musk's Terafab **explicitly targets memory** (Musk named **Micron among Terafab's existing suppliers** — "very grateful to Samsung, TSMC, Micron" — but wants them to expand faster than they are comfortable with: "we either build the terafab or we don't have the chips"). UBS flags that it is **unclear where Terafab would source memory IP and sees limited incentive for incumbents (incl. MU) to license it**; however, the emergence of another potential at-scale memory supplier — *if successful* — could ultimately **pressure incumbents** and prompt Korean players to invest in US front-end memory (more likely DRAM). A long-dated competitive-risk monitor, not a near-term thesis-mover (UBS "SpaceX's Terafab", 2026-07-07).

**Memory-market read-throughs (2026-07-06):** SK Hynix expected to **list ADRs (ticker SKHY) on Nasdaq Friday (~Jul 10)** — a memory-cycle positive that would make MU no longer the only US-listed big-3 memory maker; and **Samsung reported preliminary Q2 ahead of consensus** (sales KRW 171T vs KRW 169.2T, OI KRW 89.4T vs KRW 84.2T, via Vital Knowledge) — both reinforce the DRAM up-cycle underpinning MU's thesis (2026-07-06).

**US manufacturing investment stepped up (2026-07-10):** Micron raised its US investment plan again, now committing **~$250 billion through 2035** for US memory manufacturing (Reuters via @C_Barraud & @TrumpDailyPosts, 2026-07-10). Separately, on 2026-07-09 Micron announced a **$3B strategic US investment** including a **US$500mn prepayment to GlobalWafers (GWC, 6488 TT)** to secure 12″ silicon-wafer supply under a **10-year LTA** — MU locking up upstream US wafer supply ahead of its own capacity build (JPM / Jimmy Huang & Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-10). Nomura reads the LTA arriving **sooner than expected** as evidence (1) memory cos have ample cash to secure upstream supply early and (2) S/D is turning more favorable (upcycle underway); it raised **GWC TP to TWD1,800 (from TWD1,200), Buy**, and flags a cost read-through — LTA pricing for semi (silicon) wafers to memory customers **"could potentially double"** (semi wafers now **<1% of memory wafer price vs 3-5% two years ago**), i.e. modest silicon-wafer cost inflation inbound for makers incl. Micron (Nomura / Donnie Teng & Aaron Jeng, 2026-07-12 note; event 2026-07-09).

**Korea/Japan memory-stock correction — BofA pushes back (BofA "Global Memory Tech: DRAM ASPs look better than investor concerns", Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya, 2026-07-13):** after Korea/Japan memory names corrected sharply on fears of weak 2Q DRAM ASP / earnings misses, BofA sees **no earnings miss** — conventional-memory ASP already **+50-60% QoQ** (per Micron & Nanya results), Korea semis exports strong three straight months (Apr-Jun) and **+193% YoY in the first 10 days of July**, and DRAM spot resumed rallying since June. BofA assumes **global DRAM ASP +53% / +21% / +7% QoQ for 2Q/3Q/4Q** and stays 3Q-optimistic: **>50% of conventional DRAM is not yet under LTA** (price upside remains), HBM4 shipments are ramping and replacing cheaper HBM3e (lifting blended HBM ASP), and newly-negotiated quarterly contracts are already **+20%+ QoQ** (BofA / Simon Woo, 2026-07-13). The correction's proximate trigger was the **SK Hynix 2Q26 print landing ~8% below consensus** (OP ~W60.4tn, +61% QoQ / +556% YoY vs ~W65tn cons) — but local brokerages (KIS, Mirae) attribute the miss to SKHY's *higher HBM sales mix* (slower HBM ASP hikes vs commodity DRAM), a lower commodity-DRAM price hike vs prior Street, and — notably — the **LTA effect (more stable ASP) starting to kick in**, i.e. a smoothing artifact rather than demand weakness (@jukan05 citing KIS/Mirae, 2026-07-13/14). A read-through supportive of MU's own SCA-driven ASP-smoothing thesis rather than a cycle-turn signal. Separately, **Semiecosystem "Micron Provides Boost For U.S. Silicon Wafer Industry" (2026-07-13)** is a US-wafer-industry read-through corroborating the GlobalWafers/US-ecosystem investment logged above.

**16 Strategic Customer Agreements (SCAs) signed (from 1 last quarter) — the marquee development** (MU F3Q26 call, 2026-06-24; Barclays / Tom O'Malley, 2026-06-25): non-cancelable, take-or-pay; mostly 5-year terms (CY26–CY30), auto customers 3-year. 4 large customers, 3 medium, remainder smaller auto. Collectively cover **~20% of DRAM volume** and **~33% of NAND volume**; target **>50% of revenue** under SCAs. 14 of 16 represent a **$100B minimum revenue** floor over 5 years (ASC606 accounting floor; actual expected materially higher). GM band "well above" prior peak; UBS estimates DRAM floor price **~$6/GB** (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-06-25). A UBS follow-up reads "well above" as **~70-75% GM** (vs MU's ~62% peak in F4Q18) and pegs the SCA price band between an **implied ~$5/GB floor** and a **~$10.50/GB ceiling** (~87% GM) — with the floor not even tested until DDR falls ~75-80% from a high-teens peak, which UBS doesn't model before Aug-2029 (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-06-29). Other call disclosures: **HBM TAM raised to >$100B in CY27** (was CY28); **$5B eSSD quarter** milestone; capex increases "modest" (supply-discipline signal); capital-return pivot expected post-Dec 2026 CHIPS Act anniversary (MS / Joseph Moore, 2026-06-25). Memory market outlook from the Q3 deck: DRAM shipments 2026 +20-25% (above prior), NAND +~20% (in line); tight beyond 2027 (@ParadisLabs, 2026-06-24).

**Barron's framing of the print — "Micron Breaks the Cycle. Why the Stock Could Still Double"** (Barron's/Adam Levine, Tech Trader, 2026-06-29): FQ non-GAAP gross margin **85%**, guided to **86%** next quarter — "a 600% markup on cost of goods." CEO Sanjay Mehrotra on the F3Q26 call: **"Even as we expect industry supply to improve gradually in 2028, we currently do not have line of sight as to when memory supply will be able to catch up with increasing demand."** Price floors under the new contracts will be "well above our peak quarterly margins in any past cycle" — the 2018 cycle peak was 61% GM. Barron's counts **16 long-term take-or-pay supply contracts signed so far, ~40% of revenue once fully executed, locked through 2030**. BNP Paribas analyst Karl Ackerman: "further evidence that the memory industry is undergoing a transformative shift toward long-term supply agreements which supports demand assurance, through-cycle profitability, and mitigated cyclicality." Micron Chief Business Officer **Sumit Sadana**, to Barron's directly: "We are putting a lot of effort into not only doing these [long-term agreements] but communicating a lot of the details, and it'll take investors some time to understand all the details." Post-earnings, **35 of 42 FactSet-tracked analysts raised EPS estimates**; next-FY consensus EPS now **$144.27**, up from **$101.74 a month ago**. Barron's frames "another 100% upside" as available if the stock re-rates toward the S&P multiple from its current ~9x forward earnings (Barron's, 2026-06-29).

**Prior quarter — Q2 FY26 (reported 2026-03-18) — a blowout.** Revenue $23.9B, +75% q/q, +196% y/y (4th straight record). DRAM $18.8B (+74% q/q), pricing up "mid-sixties percent" sequentially; NAND $5.0B (+82% q/q), pricing up "high-seventies percent." Consolidated gross margin **75%** (+18pts q/q); operating margin 69%; non-GAAP EPS **$12.20**. FCF a record $6.9B; net cash $6.5B (highest ever); dividend +30% to $0.15. Supply remains the binding constraint — management can fill "only 50% to two-thirds" of key-customer demand (Mehrotra, 2026-03-18, reiterating Q1). _(F3Q26 guidance given here — $33.5B rev / ~81% GM / $19.15 EPS — was beaten by the actual print above; see Changelog.)_

**Q2 FY26 10-Q — segment economics + balance-sheet detail (10-Q, 2026-03-19, filed for qtr ended Feb-26-2026; not in the transcript already on this page):**
- **First segment-level BU breakdown (Q2 FY26 revenue / operating income, $M):** CMBU **$7,749 / $5,127** (66.2% op margin — the HBM+hyperscale unit); MCBU **$7,711 / $5,836** (75.7% — biggest swing factor, op income up from $16M y/y as mobile/client pricing turned); CDBU **$5,687 / $3,809** (67.0%); AEBU **$2,708 / $1,682** (62.1%). Total revenue $23,860M, operating income $16,135M (10-Q, 2026-03-19). _(Note: MU does not report assets or capex by segment.)_
- **y/y price/bit split (net-new vs the QoQ figures already on the page):** DRAM revenue **+207% y/y** on **+mid-110% ASP** / **+mid-40% bits**; NAND **+169% y/y** on **>100% ASP** / **~30% bits** (10-Q, 2026-03-19).
- **Deleveraging into the cash flood:** long-term debt cut to **$9.56B** from $14.02B at FY25-end; **$4.63B of notes/term-loan prepaid in 1H26** (2028 Notes, 2029 Term Loan A, 2029 A/B Notes, 2030 Notes all repaid). Cash & marketable investments **$16.63B** (vs $11.94B at FY25-end; $6.43B held abroad); $3.50B revolver fully undrawn (10-Q, 2026-03-19).
- **1H26 cash generation:** operating cash flow **$20.3B** (vs $7.2B 1H25); PP&E capex **$11.78B gross**, less **$2.26B government incentives** (~$9.5B net); capex still guided **>$25B FY26**; PP&E purchase obligations $2.10B. Capital return so far modest: $650M / 2.5M shares repurchased + $266M dividends in 1H26 (10-Q, 2026-03-19).
- **SCA-prepayment reality check (relevant to the "deposits = vendor financing" bear point):** as of Feb-26-2026 the **future performance obligations beyond one year (customer prepayments + other contract liabilities) were "not material"** — i.e. the large multi-year SCA prepayments had not yet landed on the balance sheet at Q2. What did grow was **"consideration payable to customers for pricing adjustments/returns," up to $2.55B** (from $1.19B at FY25-end) in other current liabilities (10-Q, 2026-03-19).
- **Receivables ballooning / Pillar Two tax:** receivables nearly doubled to **$17.3B** (from $9.3B) on the revenue surge — a working-capital drag to watch; inventories roughly flat at $8.27B. Effective tax rate jumped to **14.7%** (from 10.1% y/y) as Singapore's enacted Pillar Two 15% minimum tax largely offsets MU's Singapore incentive (10-Q, 2026-03-19).

**Capacity-constrained — hardest to add wafer (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** Micron is "the only company having extreme difficulty adding wafer capacity in 2026," holding ~flat at **~365K wafers/month** (vs Samsung ~740K, SK Hynix ~610K). 2027/28 growth comes from **Idaho 1 & 2 (~50-60K each, longer US construction lead times)** plus the **acquired PSMC Taiwan fab (~40K) transitioning to Micron equipment with real output H2 2027**; reaching ~440K by end-2027. Critically, Micron **grows faster than its Korean peers in 2028 (+21% YoY)** off the new greenfield. On HBM, end-2026 wafer cap **~100K, with risk of a drop to ~80K if HBM4 qualification issues persist** — though the analyst frames a weak HBM4 as paradoxically good near-term, freeing wafer back to higher-margin conventional DRAM (where Micron's Taiwan fabs run 24/7, driving a "staggering 42%" supply bit-growth) (same source).

**Price outlier — pushes the highest ASPs (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** the analyst flags **Micron as the likely price outlier** in QoQ negotiations — constrained capacity means it naturally pushes for the highest ASPs, "and if Micron succeeds, the two Korean makers will follow through" for market-share-competition reasons. (Her base-case DRAM price view is the market's most conservative: Q3 +8-13% / Q4 +3-8%, with gut-feel upside to Q3 >+20% and Q4 +10-15% — vs Conor's Redburn checks of 30-40% QoQ in both Q3 and Q4.) _Note: TrendForce revised this base case up on 2026-06-30 — see Intra-quarter log._

**Anthropic–Micron deal (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** the announced partnership is a **combination of server DRAM + enterprise SSD (2027) plus HBM (2028)**; the HBM leg "means Anthropic wants to have its own in-house ASIC." Anthropic is pursuing the same deal structure with SK Hynix and Samsung.

**Structural setup (Q1 FY26, 2025-12-17):** entire CY26 HBM supply — including HBM4 — sold out on price and volume. HBM TAM ~$35B (2025) → ~$100B by 2028 (~40% CAGR), pulled in two years vs prior. CY26 industry DRAM and NAND bit-shipment growth capped at ~20% by supply; Micron grows in line. 1γ DRAM and G9 NAND become majority bit mix by mid-CY26. First five-year Strategic Customer Agreement (SCA) signed — multiyear, specific volume/price commitments, distinct from the old one-year LTAs; in discussion with multiple customers. JPM corroborated at its TMC conference that MU emphasized supply tightness "well beyond CY26," extended undersupply through CY27, secured its first **five-year DRAM SCA** with multiple additional customers in active negotiation (incl. NAND SCAs), and that **HBM4 production is ramping ~2x faster than the HBM3E ramp**, with 1γ DRAM (EUV-based) and G9 NAND on track to be the majority bit mix by mid-2026 (JPM "Conf feedback", 2026-05-22). UBS (Arcuri) corroborates MU "sold out on HBM3E/HBM4 volumes in C2026," with industry DDR/NAND undersupply running into C1Q27 / C4Q26 respectively (UBS 2026 overview); DeepResearch TSM cross-checks Micron HBM3E "fully booked for 2026" (Sep-23-2025).

**Pricing / cycle (UBS 2026 overview, Arcuri):** the current memory upcycle "could turn out to be the longest of the last 30 years" — HBM consumes enough wafer capacity that traditional DDR wafers are flat-to-declining, structurally undersupplying DDR. UBS models DRAM contract pricing +35%/+30% q/q in C4Q25/C1Q26 and NAND +20%/+20%, with customers likely accepting double-digit sequential ASP increases through C2026. Key call: MU's DDR (ex-HBM) gross margin likely **surpasses HBM GM for the first time in early C2026** as DDR tightens. Into the June print, Arcuri's incremental tell was that the market is fixated on DDR but **HBM pricing is "what is really surprising to the upside"** — and that the LTAs (not pricing) are the re-rating catalyst; MU itself told him after the PT raise "we think the stock should trade at a higher multiple and you're finally the one that opened this debate" (UBS "Arcuri call", 2026-06-15). JPM separately models blended HBM ASP **+~30% for 2026** (~10% like-for-like + mix from HBM4) — but with HBM4 ramp pushed to 2H, more of the ASP effect spills into 2027; HBM price negotiations only begin July after the June quarter (JPM "Memory", 2026-05-28). Jefferies expert checks (Inspectrum, 2025-12-17) add HBM contract prices are signed and better than feared — HBM4 ASP a ~60% premium vs prior assumption of HBM3E declining ~20%; HBM industry bit production ~+50% in 2026 (≈20bn→30bn 1Gb-eq). 2026 WFE intentions skew to DRAM (the strongest segment) over NAND, with NAND capacity roughly flat (Jefferies/TEL, 2025-12-17). BofA's post-Korea-conference read (2026-06-21/22) adds a forward twist: **chipmakers intend to raise HBM prices aggressively in 2027** — because (1) conventional DRAM price has now exceeded HBM, (2) strong HBM4/4e orders, and (3) HBM cost is still several times DDR5 cost — corroborating the "DDR GM surpasses HBM" inversion. BofA raises 2027-28E global DRAM sales 10-15% (8-12% higher ASP, mostly HBM/SOCAMM) and sees the **global memory market topping ~$1.0tn in 2027** (DRAM ~$0.8tn / NAND ~$0.4tn). BofA industry model: DRAM sales +309%/+42% (26E/27E), DRAM ASP +233%/+24%, DRAM bit +23%/+15%; NAND ASP +228%/+7% (BofA "Global Memory Tech — PO lifts", 2026-06-21).

**Micron IR call — the most complete SCA disclosure yet, and management walks the tightness horizon forward a third time (Micron IR call · Satish, 2026-07-27).** The second IR call Micron has hosted for a Brazilian institutional-investor audience. Several items sharpen numbers already on this page rather than adding new ones — noted where so:
- **Guidance restated:** Aug-quarter **86% gross margin**, "an increase from the quarter we reported before, which was approximately 85%." On capital: Micron expects "that we will be able to **return 100% of our excess cash to shareholders**" over time.
- **The tightness horizon keeps extending.** Management's own framing has moved **2026 → 2027 (post-earnings) → "beyond calendar 27 and even in 28."** The starkest formulation: "based on our visibility, **we actually do not see a line of sight to when the supply demand balance even comes back into balance**." The *walk-forward itself* is the signal — the level has been revised out at every one of the last three touchpoints. Asked what could normalize the market earlier, Satish pointed to the structural supply governors already on this page (greenfield build time, new nodes not producing as much bit growth, HBM consuming capacity) and to demand "far in excess of supply."
- **SCA architecture, quantified end-state — and it reconciles two figures this page carried side by side.** As of earnings, **25% of revenue committed across 16 contracts, 14 of which carry a pricing framework.** When all planned SCAs are done: **up to 50%+ of total company revenue under SCAs**, split **~40% with a pricing framework (price band or fixed price) + ~10% volume-only take-or-pay with pricing negotiated quarterly (as today)**, leaving **~50% market-priced**. "Probably today that number is in the low 20%" for the framework tranche. _This resolves the page's apparent conflict between management's ">50% of revenue under SCAs" target and Barron's "~40% of revenue once fully executed" — they are the same plan measured on different tranches, not two estimates._ **All SCAs carry take-or-pay volume commitments** — "volume is the key thing that we need to plan our supply."
- **The RPO caveat — it is a floor, not a forecast, and management said so unprompted.** The **~$100bn RPO** across the 14 priced contracts is computed as **contract volume × the FLOOR price**. Satish: "the pricing is at the **ceiling** right now… the revenue that we will actually get on the SCAs, we expect will be **well above the RPO levels**. The RPO is not indicative of the revenue that we will get." Band mechanics: **ceiling ≈ the market price in the June calendar quarter** — "roughly consistent with the type of pricing that we were getting in our results and guidance," i.e. the ceiling is embedded in the 86% GM guide; **floor GM "well above any quarterly cyclical peak gross margin"**, where the prior peak cited is **fiscal 2018, "in the low 60% range"** — "we haven't quantified that level, but we have said it's well above." The band applies for the **entire contract duration** (five years if a five-year deal) on the current product portfolio; a minority of SCAs are fixed-price throughout. On the residual ~50% at market pricing, Micron confirmed it embeds its own price view to get to the "up to 50% or more" claim but "we don't provide a forecast of the revenue of the pricing."
- **China exposure has roughly halved:** from the **high-20s%** two years ago to the **low-double-digit %** today. Two drivers, both named: **Chinese-government restrictions on some Chinese data-center companies buying Micron products**, and Micron's own decision **not to participate in the NAND-flash portion of the mobile market**. Remaining China customers are in **automotive and robotics**.
- **The technology-leadership defence against CXMT/YMTC, given on CXMT's Shanghai debut day.** Micron is **~3 technology nodes ahead** of the Chinese competitors; **1-gamma DRAM (EUV-based) will be more than half of DRAM production around this time of year**, with more EUV use at the next node, **1-delta** — "technologies that are not available outside of in China." Plus **~65,000 patents accumulated over nearly 50 years** and "a historical track record of aggressively defending our patents when we need to." Historical rebuttal offered: two years ago the identical China-supply fear (LPDDR4 into smartphones) was followed by "a historic gap between demand and supply, even with additional capacity that has come from China-based companies."
- **On memory-as-a-commodity:** "the world is actually not black or white… we are increasingly seeing portions of our portfolio where our products are already behaving in a different way" — HBM has had annual volume-and-price contracts for three years with no spot/quarterly market, automotive likewise, and the SCAs now broaden that structure across customers. On double-ordering, Micron says it triangulates customer forecasts against its own market estimate (project tracking, memory intensity per gigawatt, third-party forecasts) rather than taking forecasts at face value. (Micron IR call · Satish, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_27_mu_ir_call_27_jul_26.html)

## HBM technology / share (deep dive)
- **MU's HBM positioning:** Micron leapfrogged in HBM — it skipped standard HBM3 entirely — on the back of a focus on TSVs and the power-delivery network, underpinning its claimed ~30% lower power consumption (claim **explicitly unverified** — SemiAnalysis flags this as an unverified claim, not confirmed) (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.7-8). Micron and Samsung use Non-Conductive Film (NCF) / thermal-compression bonding (TCB) vs Hynix's proprietary MR-MUF molded underfill (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.6). For MU and SK, yield loss on HBM is more than offset by pricing → HBM is margin-accretive; Samsung's worse yields ironically tighten total supply (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.8).
- **HBM4 node choice:** Micron is staying on the **1b node for HBM4** — the same node as SK Hynix — rather than jumping to a new node the way Samsung is attempting with 1c (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.47). Micron **lacks internal advanced-logic design capability** and needs silicon partners/TSMC for the HBM4 custom base die (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.26) — consistent with the base-die logic-gap point below.
- **AMD supply-share shift:** AMD has shifted a significant share of its HBM3E supply **to Micron and away from Samsung** (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall," 2025-08-11, p.46) — a customer-level share data point supporting MU's #2-3 HBM positioning independent of the TAM/pricing commentary elsewhere on this page.
- **HBM4 base die — the key watch item:** the HBM4 base die moves to a modern logic process (TSMC N12 and N3 variants). Pure-memory makers Hynix and Micron lack leading-edge logic design capability and will lean on TSMC + IP partners; only Samsung can build the advanced base die in-house. Custom base dies arrive with HBM4 for NVIDIA/AMD, broadening to other accelerators at HBM4E (~2027); they strengthen vendor lock-in because each is tied to a vendor's proprietary TSV layout (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-12). Caution flag: Jefferies expert (Inspectrum, 2025-12-17) says **Micron is redesigning its HBM4 base die** to reach speed parity — the two Korean players (esp. Samsung) carry higher confidence on higher speeds, i.e. MU has work to do on the new base die.
- **HBM4 pin-speed execution risk — SemiAnalysis skepticism (net-new, sharper than the Jefferies caution above):** MU's HBM4 samples showed **"much lower pin speeds"** than Samsung/SK Hynix (~10 Gbps); SemiAnalysis is explicitly **"skeptical of execution"** despite management's public confidence, and forecasts **~0% Micron share of Rubin HBM supply in the first 12 months** (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06, p.29). This is a materially more cautious read than the "redesigning for speed parity" framing already on this page and should be weighed as the more bearish end of the HBM4-execution range.
- **★ THE FIRST SUPPLY-CHAIN CHECK ON WHETHER THE PIN-SPEED SKEPTICISM ABOVE WAS RIGHT — and it lands between the two poles (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03).** **Verbatim: "from the Rubin HBM side, in the supply chain, we currently only see a SMALL VOLUME OF MICRON AND SAMSUNG'S HBM. And the Rubin with Hynix HBM4 will start to ramp up at LATE AUGUST OR SEPTEMBER WITH A BIGGER VOLUME."** ⚠️ **Read it as a qualification-and-volume statement, not a share statement: MU is *in* — which is better than SemiAnalysis's ~0%-of-first-12-months forecast on the line above — but it is not the vendor pacing the ramp, and Fubon explicitly ties Rubin's back-half weighting (200-300K units in Q3 → 700-800K in Q4) to SK Hynix HBM4 arriving. The honest framing for the model: MU has the design win and is shipping, the volume curve belongs to [[SKHYNIX]], and the gap is the thing to track through Sep-Oct.** **Same call, the execution flag that partly sits inside the HBM stack rather than at NVDA: the ~two-month Rubin production push-out is attributed to "the thermal design change AND THE HBM BASE DIE ISSUE" — the base die being the element MU sources from TSMC (N12/N3) rather than building in-house, per the base-die bullet above.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_sherman_update_03_08_26.html)
- **Fab-timeline detail (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06):** the **A3 fab (Idaho)** is predominantly HBM-allocated; its ramp has been pulled in from 2H27 to **mid-2027**, with wafer output more likely **3Q27** (p.23, 30). The newly acquired **P5 Tongluo fab** (acquired from PSMC) is unlikely to show meaningful output until **2H27**; max capacity ~**45k wafers/month** for commodity DRAM at full utilization, potentially lower if configured for HBM (p.23, 30). SemiAnalysis expects **~30% of DRAM production on the 1c/1γ node by end-2026** (p.24), and pegs **2026 DRAM WFE capex growth at +20%** — the lowest of the three memory makers' capex growth rates (p.34).
- **The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off — the single most useful supplier-side datapoint of the AMD Advancing AI cycle (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** AMD's MI455X ships **12 stacks of HBM4 = 432GB per package** and **23.3 TB/s** of memory bandwidth vs **8 stacks / 288GB** on Nvidia Rubin and Google, with Rubin at **22 TB/s** — a near-tie on bandwidth *despite AMD running a 50% wider bus*, because AMD clocks its HBM4 at a pin speed of **7.6 Gbps while Nvidia targets 10.7 Gbps (40% faster)**. SemiAnalysis is explicit that this was a deliberate, supplier-facing decision: "**Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification. This change was precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X.**" The consequence lands directly on the memory makers: the change "**was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but as of today these issues have been finally resolved**" — and the standing implication is that **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD** for the same HBM4 generation. AMD and Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. **Read-through for Micron:** an above-JEDEC pin-speed target means tighter binning, lower effective yield on the top bin, and a widening quality/price spread between suppliers that can hit 10.7 Gbps and those that cannot — a **share-and-mix question, not a volume question**, and the axis on which this page's standing HBM4 pin-speed skepticism (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06: MU samples at "much lower pin speeds," ~0% modeled Rubin share yr-1) is most likely to be resolved either way. _SemiAnalysis does not say which supplier wins the top bin — do not read one in._ Note it also implies a **two-tier HBM4 market by construction**: the AMD 7.6 Gbps socket is a materially easier bin than the Nvidia 10.7 Gbps socket, so a supplier short of Nvidia's spec is not shut out of HBM4 — it is shut out of the *premium* bin. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
  - _Dual-source corroboration:_ the same note's Microsoft/MI300X history (Microsoft dropped AMD after MI300X over "unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM memory") corroborates — rather than adds to — the AMD-shifted-HBM3E-share-to-Micron datapoint already on this page from "Scaling the Memory Wall" (2025-08-11); the current AMD dual-source setup stands, no new share number.
  - _Verification-complexity read-through (one line, different source):_ Micron is named among the memory companies using specialised DRAM/NAND/HBM design tools, where **HBM verification now approaches logic-chip complexity as stacking and interposer-routing requirements grow** — a cost/cycle-time input consistent with the base-die/logic-gap point above (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **HBM share (UBS estimates, 2026 overview):** MU HBM bit shipment 0.9 / 4.6 / 7.4 bn Gb (2024/25/26E); HBM as % of MU total DRAM bits 1.5% / 6.2% / 8.4%; MU HBM bit-share of the market ~21-22% in 2026E (vs SK Hynix the leader). MU HBM front-end capacity 30 / 65 / 100 k-wpm (4Q, '24/'25/'26E). Samsung's 2025 HBM share ~20%, "will go up next year" (Jefferies/Inspectrum, 2025-12-17). UBS: MU's HBM business alone supports **$5 / $7+ of EPS in 2026/2027** (~17-18% of EPS), justifying a higher multiple on the segment.

- **Agentic-AI memory-hierarchy sizing (UBS teach-in with MU mgmt, 2026-06-08):** a single agentic prompt can spawn ~4-5 simultaneous agents (each a separate CPU task interfacing with GPU/ASIC), and **query context grows ~30x/yr** — a multiplicative step-up in DRAM capacity, HBM bandwidth, and SSD storage per query. MU quantified the tiers: **HBM-resident active KV cache ~10-100GB/GPU at ~1-20TB/s** (residency ms-to-s); next tier **~200-400GB KV cache/GPU on DRAM at ~100-500GB/s** (s-to-min); **NAND "context memory" tens-to-hundreds of TB** (warm data / vector DB). Products: 192GB SOCAMM already shipping in volume + newly announced 256GB SOCAMM and 256GB RDIMM (**~3x DRAM capacity per CPU socket** vs prior gen); 245TB SSD (rack-level → multi-petabyte AI storage). MU's stance on efficiency (TurboQuant, sparse attention, quantization): gains get reinvested into larger context windows / more agents rather than cutting hardware content (UBS "FQ3:26 preview / agentic teach-in", 2026-06-08). UBS's $1,625 PT = **~15x NTM P/E on C2029E EPS ~$124-125, discounted back** (C2029 is UBS's through-cycle anchor, assuming a moderate downcycle buoyed by LTAs).

- **HBM4/HBM4E primer — MU's place in the 3-supplier universe (Atlas Peak Research "HBM4 and HBM4E: The Memory Wall Becomes the System Architecture Problem," 2026-04-03):** JEDEC finalized HBM4 in April 2025 (2048-bit interface / 32 channels / 2.048 TB/s per stack at the 8 Gb/s baseline — ~2x HBM3E's 1024-bit/1.229 TB/s); HBM4E extends to 16 Gb/s / ~4.096 TB/s (~2027 for SK hynix/Samsung). Micron-specific: **shipped HBM4 to key customers in June 2025 and moved into high-volume HBM4 production for NVIDIA Vera Rubin in March 2026 (36 GB 12-Hi)**; **HBM4E timeline extends to ~2028** (later than the Koreans' ~2027); uses an **in-house advanced CMOS base die** for HBM4 and **custom base dies with TSMC** for HBM4E — Micron has said HBM4E with **optional base-die customization will carry higher gross margin than standard HBM4E**; its **HBM customer base has expanded to 6 customers**. Value capture is shifting into the logic base die, advanced packaging and substrate (ABF ~100% share), not only DRAM bits (Atlas Peak, 2026-04-03).

## NAND / storage
- **AI finally reaches NAND (MS, 2025-09-10):** incremental ~$29B AI-inference NAND TAM by 2029; AI NAND ≈34% of global NAND market by 2029. Industry shifts from oversupply (since 2022) to **shortage from 2H26** — ~2% base case, up to ~8% in the bull case as nearline (NL) SSD takes share from NL HDD amid HDD capacity constraints. QLC eSSD is the optimal storage tier for AI (high-capacity, randomized I/O). MS prefers SNDK / Korea names directly; read-through to MU is positive but MU is not MS's NAND top pick. Constructive for MU's ~20% NAND mix into the back half of CY26.

## Debate / thesis
- **LTA/SCA structural-shift thesis — flagged explicitly as a major bull/bear debate item.** Bulls (BNP Paribas' Ackerman via Barron's, 2026-06-29; sell-side broadly, see below) read the 16 take-or-pay contracts (~40% of revenue once fully executed, locked through 2030) as evidence the memory industry itself is "undergoing a transformative shift toward long-term supply agreements which supports demand assurance, through-cycle profitability, and mitigated cyclicality" — i.e. it de-risks the cyclicality that has always capped memory-stock multiples. Bears/skeptics flag the *newness* of the dynamic as the risk, not the reassurance: a SemiAnalysis reader comment (Drew Van Pelt, "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06, p.35 — explicitly a comment, not the SemiAnalysis analyst body) notes the current LTAs are "very different from the past," i.e. a structural break from history that the market/analysts have limited precedent to model — cuts both ways: it could mean the cyclicality is genuinely gone, or it could mean investors are underwriting duration/terms that have never been tested through a real downturn. MU's own Chief Business Officer Sumit Sadana concedes the communication gap directly: "We are putting a lot of effort into not only doing these [long-term agreements] but communicating a lot of the details, and it'll take investors some time to understand all the details" (Sadana to Barron's, 2026-06-29) — i.e. even management frames this as a still-developing narrative, not a fully priced-in fact. **Bernstein (Mark Newman) "New Memory LTAs part 2 - Case studies from Microchip" (2026-07-09)** puts the sharpest historical frame on the deposits-as-downside-protection question: Micron backs its memory-LTA obligations with **$18B of cash deposits on its own balance sheet plus $4B in letters of credit**, and — because these upfront guarantees are back-end-weighted, with the **coverage ratio rising toward 75-100% of remaining obligations in the outer years** — they give **"meaningful but not infinite" downside protection**, materially better-structured than the old semiconductor LTAs that broke through prior downturns (Microchip's PSP, Hemlock polysilicon). This is the constructive counter to the "deposits = vendor financing" bear point (@PatrickMoorhead) and the "never tested through a real downturn" skeptic read (Bernstein "New Memory LTAs part 2", 2026-07-09).
- **Bernstein "Memory LTA effectiveness" — the skeptic's quantitative bound on the same LTA debate (Bernstein · Mark Newman, 2026-07-09).** A separate, same-day Bernstein cut (distinct from the constructive "part 2 — Case studies from Microchip" note above; both Newman, 2026-07-09) puts the harder numbers on *how much* protection the deposits actually buy — quantifying the "not infinite" side of the "meaningful but not infinite" conclusion. Bernstein argues LTA effectiveness is **impossible to quantify precisely** (the floor price, market price and the customer's walk-away barrier are all time-dependent, and the barrier *rises* as RPO amortizes). Top-down: the **~$3.33B of deposits secured industry-wide so far is <1% (~0.6%) of the revenue an LTA book would need to protect**; a genuinely "secure" **20% deposit would require ~$1 trillion of customer cash upfront** — "who on earth has that kind of cash today?" On the marquee **Micron–Anthropic deal specifically:** Micron gave a supply guarantee and Anthropic gave back **an equity stake, not cash** — Bernstein calls this **"vendor financing" that does NOT reduce risk for investors** (a sharper, named version of @PatrickMoorhead's generic "deposits = vendor financing" bear). And the **ceiling of LTA coverage is only ~50-67% of the market** (SanDisk's CEO: PC/smartphone "customer-customers" are transactional and won't sign; China customers prefer increasingly-large local suppliers), so a third-to-half of demand stays uncovered. Bear-leaning on the LTA-durability thesis (Bernstein "Memory LTA effectiveness", 2026-07-09). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_09_bernstein_on_lta_s.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_09_bernstein_on_lta_s.html)
- **Price up, content down — the 2027 HBM-pricing call and the offsetting de-spec, from the buyer's own BOM (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The bull leg first: SemiAnalysis explicitly expects "**a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM**" — i.e. commodity-DRAM margin is now the *anchor* HBM is being repriced toward, the exact inversion this page already tracks (UBS: DDR GM surpassing HBM GM in early C2026; BofA 06-21: chipmakers intend to raise HBM prices aggressively in 2027). It is modelled as strong enough to **compress the accelerator vendors' own margins**: AMD's ASPs must rise but it cannot pass the full memory increase through, because "most of AMD's volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage, and those buyers have more alternatives than they used to," so HBM cost increases pass through "**at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package.**" Baseline context: "**HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025**" (SemiAnalysis Memory Model / Accelerator and HBM Model). **The bear leg is the same note and is the reason a straight price × prior-content revenue model is wrong.** Tight memory supply is already destroying attach: AMD's original 2025 BOM estimate for the MI455X platform carried "a staggering **138TB in DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM**"; the current estimate has **LPDDR5X entirely removed** plus a **significant DDR5 de-spec to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM content** — specifically the previously-roadmapped **up to 1TB of direct-attached LPDDR per MI455X accelerator on the EAM module has disappeared**, which SemiAnalysis attributes directly to tight memory supply. Its framing is the part to carry: "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" **Why this matters here:** it is the mechanism behind MS/Joe Moore's 07-24 "LTAs and de-speccing could flatten the amplitude of the cycle… but increase duration" line already on this page — now with a named platform and a quantified content cut behind it, sourced from the buyer's BOM rather than from a memory maker. Consensus revenue models that take 2027 HBM/DRAM price up while holding per-accelerator content flat are double-counting; the honest read is **price-led revenue with content as a live offset**. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Historical valuation context (Barron's Streetwise, Jack Hough, June 1 2026 issue):** "A year and a half ago... Micron... was actually the cheapest stock in America relative to earnings. It was about $540 at the start of May [2026]... Now the stock is over $900, and Micron this past week passed a trillion-dollar market value." The same issue's cover story "Rally in Chips" (Barron's/Adam Levine) added: Micron "saw 196% year-over-year sales growth with a 74% gross margin in the most recent quarter... adjusted EPS will grow from $8.29 to over $100 in fiscal 2027... at a recent $924, the stock is trading at just nine times 2027 EPS." Debt reduced 33% over the past three quarters; buying back shares for the first time since 2022. Tech Trader (Adam Levine, same issue) on the supply-lockup dynamic: "Micron, SK Hynix, and Samsung Electronics... don't have significant new manufacturing coming on-line until the middle of next year [2027]... All of this year's supply is spoken for, and 2027 order books are getting close to full" (Barron's, 2026-06-01).
- **Bull:** This is a durable up-cycle, not a normal one. DB (Melissa Weathers, Buy) raised PT 2026-06-17: "DRAM tightness extends to 2028+; agentic AI drives memory intensity." GS (Schneider) sees "ongoing tightness through CY27; significant upside to Street estimates" (briefing 2026-06-09). The marquee bull is **UBS Arcuri (Buy, PT $1,625, raised from $535)** on a "path to >$100 EPS," LTAs locking ~30% of industry DDR bits and ~$400bn FCF C27-29E (briefings 2026-05-26 / 2026-05-31). Citi (Atif Malik, Buy, PT $840) re-rated to 8x C27 EPS from 5x (2026-05-19); MS reiterates bull case PT $1,050 (2026-06-04). Mechanism: HBM's 3:1 DDR5 die trade-off + node-driven bit decline + multi-year cleanroom lead times keep supply structurally short while LTAs/SCAs convert the equity into an earnings-durability story (JPM memory-China call, 2026-05-28). The UBS 2026 deck adds two structural legs: HBM cannibalizing DDR wafer capacity keeps DDR undersupplied (DDR GM may surpass HBM GM in early C2026), and the longest memory upcycle in 30 years. CPU upside is incremental fuel — NVIDIA Vera standalone CPU ~500k units this year, ~3M next year, could lift total server DRAM demand >15-20% (JPM/Gokul, 2026-05-28).
- **Bear:** Memory is still a commodity and the cycle always turns. The sharpest bear voice on disk is the **ex-VP Procurement (Micron)**, 2026-06-10: the market is pricing peak earnings as a sustainable level — "a highly catastrophic assumption." His view: HBM differentiation is temporary (once a second/third vendor is qualified at each customer it commoditizes like DRAM), Micron's cost structure is worse than Samsung's and roughly at SK Hynix, and "your $900 stock right now will become a $50 stock again" once new fabs (Micron/SK/Samsung) bring disruptive capacity online ~a year out — possibly normalizing "within this calendar year or early next." Two technical bear reads-through support the "differentiation is temporary" thesis: MU is redesigning its HBM4 base die for speed parity and lacks in-house leading-edge logic for custom base dies (Jefferies/Inspectrum + SemiAnalysis) — Samsung's foundry is a structural edge there. He frames the AI buildout as a bull-whip ("beer game") several layers removed from end demand that can't amortize ~$6-700B of infra spend. Second-order: customers are being forced to qualify Chinese suppliers (CXMT) on cheap DRAM Micron won't supply — "once they're in, they're in." UBS's own desk called the Arcuri PT "the most controversial UBS note recently" (briefing 2026-05-31).
- **Buy-side voice — Alex Chaloff (CIO, Bernstein Private Wealth Management), named PM Q&A, Barron's 2026-06-22:** "We are focused on Seagate Technology Holdings and Micron Technology, which have seen over 200% growth this year on the back of significant pickup in spend on memory" — a named, on-record buy-side CIO citing MU (alongside STX) as a core memory-boom holding, corroborating the sell-side consensus from the demand side rather than a bank's own research seat (Barron's/Chaloff, 2026-06-22; see also [STX.md](STX.md) cross-reference).
- **Barron's frames the two-sided debate explicitly (2026-07-14/15):** the bull cut **"The Case for Micron Stock Rising Nearly 90%"** (@barronsonline, 2026-07-14) vs the bear cut **"Micron Stock Falls With Chinese Competition About to Get Fiercer"** (@barronsonline, 2026-07-15) — mainstream-press bookends on the page's core duration debate: the upside is the SCA-supported re-rate, the downside is exactly the accelerating China DRAM capacity (CXMT + Huawei/SwaySure) logged in Risks. The incremental new-news this week is on the long-dated China-supply risk axis, not the near-term cycle. (Barron's, 2026-07-14/15)
- **Where the sell-side stands:** Near-unanimous bullish — DB Buy (raised PT, 2026-06-17), GS PT up (2026-06-09), MS PT $1,050 (2026-06-04), UBS Arcuri Buy $1,625 (2026-05-26/31), Citi Buy $840 (2026-05-19), Susquehanna $1,750 (2026-05-30), JPM OW (TMC recap, 2026-05-21). MU +30% on the week and +9.9% in a single session into June (briefings). The debate is no longer direction but duration — the bull case explicitly underwrites tightness "beyond CY26" / into 2028; the bear case is that this is a textbook top.
- **Where the sell-side stands — post-F3Q26 print (2026-06-25):** uniform PT increases on the beat-and-raise + SCA expansion.

"Substantial Beat-and-Raise and Major SCA Expansion Anchor a Higher and More Durable Earnings Floor; Capital Return Pivot Post-Dec 2026 Adds Another Value Unlock Lever" — SCA step-change "fundamentally transforms MU's business model from cyclical commodity producer to multi-year contracted supplier" (JPM / Harlan Sur, 2026-06-25).
  - **BofA (Vivek Arya):** Buy; PO **$1,500→$1,550**. "Another memorable beat, SCA durability, FCF optionality"; ~10% FCF yield; estimates raised 4-7%; "price durability could structurally rerate memory stocks" toward 12-15x P/E from 8-10x historical (BofA / Vivek Arya, 2026-06-25).

Citi's Aug-quarter sales/EPS are **above Street** (Citi / Atif Malik, 2026-07-06).
  - **UBS (Tim Arcuri):** Buy (maintained $1,625). "Setting The Stage For Re-Rating"; SCA floor ~$6/GB DRAM pushes oversupply impact to C2029/C2030 earliest (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-06-25). _UBS callback specifics (2026-06-25):_ guidance implies open-market DDR **~$13/GB** in the Aug guide (going higher beyond); SCA **ceiling ≈$10-11/GB** (≈CQ1 pricing), **floor ≈$5-6/GB** (70-75% GM on ~$1.25/GB cost) — so customers pay the ceiling until open-market breaches it, then quarterly negotiation. **Some HBM travels inside the SCAs** (surprising, given shifting XPU roadmaps); by contrast the Koreans' 5-yr deals exclude HBM. Prepayments should roughly scale to ~$40-50B as SCA coverage doubles from ~25%→50%+ of revenue (today $22B = $18B cash + $4B LC); Arcuri treats this as a **de-facto break fee**. RPO in the 10-K will be booked at the floor price, so **~2x it** to gauge real revenue capacity. Buyback (post Dec-9-2026 CHIPS sunset): UBS models **~$143B in calendar-2027 and ~$200B in calendar-2028** (~$350B cumulative; ~$15B in the Feb-27 quarter scaling to ~$40-50B/qtr) — could retire ~30-35% of the company over two years; ~$75-80B cash on hand by Dec-9. **FY27 capex now low-to-mid $50s** (UBS model $50-52B, up from the low-40s steer last call; ~$5B calendarized WFE add); MU steering the **cost curve up not down** (HBM mix + pricier greenfield bits). Normalized through-cycle margin could exceed **50%** (half the company at ~75% SCA floor, other half ~35% 2015-25 median GM) → supports a higher book multiple (UBS / Tim Arcuri callback, 2026-06-25).

"Making the Case for Another Leg Higher"; SCAs/NBMs shift memory from commodity cyclicality "toward something closer to a recurring revenue profile." **Downside-floor math:** holding pricing/COGS flat with bits +20% Y/Y, MU's combined DRAM+NAND GM lands **mid-70s%** → CY28 EPS **~$75**; at 10x = **~$750 floor** (~16% downside) — a proxy trough that supports an earnings multiple. Note HBM is currently **dilutive** to MU's corporate-average GM (elevated DDR5 pricing) but management expects HBM structurally margin-accretive through cycle. March-qtr DRAM $/GB **+113% Y/Y** for MU (Barclays / Tom O'Malley, 2026-05-26).

Sizes the **HBM TAM at $36B→$246B (90% 2025-28E CAGR)** — well above the ~$100B-by-CY27 consensus — with **2027E HBM4/4e blended pricing +70-100% y/y** and some ASIC customers jumping straight from HBM3e to HBM4e; **HBM trade ratio 4:1** (vs the 3:1 elsewhere on this page). Agentic AI adds **9-13% incremental global DRAM demand in 2027E** (bottom-up / BoE / top-down), potentially lifting DRAM growth to 30%+ y/y (vs prior ~22%). NVIDIA Vera carries **3x LPDDR5 content vs Grace** (500GB→1.5TB SOCAMM); a Vera CPU-only rack ~400TB DRAM, ~7.9k such racks ≈ 3EB (~7% of 2026E supply). MU also potentially developing **HBF** (16-layer stacked SLC, 8-16x capacity). Key customers receiving only **50-70%** of estimated demand (Mizuho / Vijay Rakesh, 2026-05-27; header-dated May-27 vs manifest Jun-03).
  - **MS (Joseph Moore) — pre-print PT origin (2026-06-03):** OW; PT **$520→$1,050** (the raise later stepped to $1,200 post-print). Methodology = **~29.5x through-cycle EPS of $35** (a small premium to the FY21-28 average $34); bull/base/bear **$1,650 / $1,050 / $675** (33x/29.5x/27x through-cycle EPS of $50/$35/$25). Raised CY26/CY27 EPS **+4%/+48%** on higher pricing; models DRAM +40% in the May Q and +15% in Aug. Frames LTAs as **"a symptom not a cause"** of higher-for-longer. Models **$50bn of buybacks across FY27-28** (MU largely restricted until the CHIPS Act provision sunsets); FY27/28 capex **$44bn/$40bn**, well above consensus $37.6bn/$36.9bn (MS / Joseph Moore, 2026-06-03).
  - **Barclays (Tom O'Malley):** OW. "New SCA Details Lend Confidence to Growth Story"; take-or-pay across 16 customers (DC/Consumer/Auto), "pricing upside on new products" still possible (Barclays / Tom O'Malley, 2026-06-25).
  - **Fubon/Jefferies (Richard Hsia):** Positive — "Positive interpretation of Micron guidance, SCA information"; GM floor "at least above 60%" (Fubon Securities / Richard Hsia, 2026-06-25).

  - **UBS (Nicolas Gaudois) — Buy, PT US$1,625 reiterated (UBS "Memory Semis Monthly", 2026-07-03):** in the July '26 monthly UBS keeps **Buy, PT US$1,625** on MU, and draws the key LTA-structure distinction: **Micron's SCAs use a price "floor" and "ceiling"** whereas the Korean makers fix ~**60-70% of volume at fixed pricing over a 5-year contract** (with a variable volume/price component layered on) — corroborating the SCA-band framing already densely covered on this page from the UBS/Arcuri callbacks. Industry backdrop: DRAM DDR contract pricing raised to **+32% QoQ 3Q26 (from +17%)** and **+18% 4Q26 (from +12%)** after +67% in 2Q26; DRAM undersupplied **until at least 2Q28** (2027 sufficiency ratio -13.6% vs -8.1% 2026E); memory-industry revenue **$992bn 2026 / $1,763bn 2027**; HBM demand **33.1bn Gb 2026 (+90% YoY) / 58.7bn Gb 2027 (+77%)** (UBS "Memory Semis Monthly", 2026-07-03). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_memory.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_memory.html)
  - **Bernstein (David Dai) — Outperform, PT US$1,300; DRAM the first High-NA EUV adopter (Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06):** in a note primarily on ASML, Bernstein carries **MU at Outperform, PT US$1,300** (7.7x forward 5Q-8Q EPS of US$169.8; note ticker table shows adjusted-EPS basis, 2027E EPS US$158.99). The memory read-through: because DRAM die is much smaller than logic and uses **1 mask (no stitching)**, the cost of High-NA (HNA) EUV exposure is cheaper/easier to justify for DRAM — so **DRAM will likely adopt HNA first, in 2027 at the 1d node**, ahead of logic (Intel 2028/A14, TSMC ~2030/A10). Bernstein's best guess for the DRAM order: **Samsung/Hynix DRAM in 2027 (1d)**, with **Micron adopting slightly later, consistent with its more conservative EUV/LNA strategy in DRAM** — a mild relative-laggard flag for MU on leading-edge litho adoption, though not a thesis-changer for the PT (Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML__High-NA_EUV_cost_deep_dive_-_lifting_PT_to_2300_on_ri.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML__High-NA_EUV_cost_deep_dive_-_lifting_PT_to_2300_on_ri.html)

With AI now the principal driver of memory bits and little-to-no inventory across the supply chain, MS frames the **biggest risk as a broad AI-capex slowdown — which it does not expect to peak near-term** — supporting a path to **higher earnings power well beyond 2027** (when consensus still assumes pricing normalizes). MS expects continued upward estimate revisions, and at **<10x P/E for both MU and SNDK** sees room for multiples to benefit from extended visibility; LTAs enable that visibility and are "symptomatic of a market that could/should stay tight for another 2-3 years." Both MU and SanDisk should remain **attractive capital-return stories with meaningful buybacks in 2027**. MS order-of-preference PT: **$1,200** (bull $1,650 / bear $675) (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook", 2026-07-02). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html)
