First TD Cowen datapoint on the page (TD Cowen "Earnings Preview: Puts & Takes", 2026-07-09).
  - **Mizuho (Vijay Rakesh) — Outperform, PT $1,375 (Mizuho "DC Capex Ramping to >$1.4T in 2028E", 2026-07-11):** raised from **$1,150 (2026-05-27 — see Changelog)**; px $979.30. In a DC-capex industry note Mizuho names MU among the AI-DC-capex "technology enablers" and "critical suppliers," with the thesis that consensus AI data-center capex reaches **$1.2T in 2027E (+35% y/y) / >$1.4T in 2028E (+12% y/y)**, and ~$50B/GW across a cumulative **106GW deployed 2026-30E** implies **>$5.3T** of AI spend (~4x combined 2025-26E) — while **component (memory) prices "remain elevated,"** the explicit positive for MU/SNDK. [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/DC_Capex_Ramping_to__14T_in_2028E_FCF_Outlook_Improving.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/DC_Capex_Ramping_to__14T_in_2028E_FCF_Outlook_Improving.html)
- **Longevity-of-cycle re-rating (GS, Junie Yoon):** GS's core argument is that the **longevity of the cycle is underestimated** — Hynix/Samsung (and by read-across MU) trading at 5-6x earnings says the market doesn't believe the durability; GS's own 9x target is "conservative" vs semis peers with similar margins/moats trading high-teens-to-20x. The downside-protection math: even a **bear case of DRAM pricing −30% in 2027 then −30% in 2028 (or −50% in 2028) still leaves DRAM gross margin ~40%** — supporting an earnings-based multiple. The lower sharp-downturn risk vs prior cycles is because demand visibility (esp. hyperscaler) lets suppliers spend capex efficiently rather than overbuild (GS "Memory", 2026-06-08).
- **DRAM > NAND, MU the cheaper 2028 play (MS, Joe Moore):** MS frames the fundamentals "10 out of 10 for DRAM, maybe 8/10 for NAND" and is **more bullish on Micron than SanDisk** — DRAM is the primary AI bottleneck (NVIDIA halved LPDDR5 content in Vera Rubin racks because there isn't enough memory; servers shipping "significantly nerfed" on DRAM, so the DRAM shortage is worse than the CPU shortage). MS views Micron as "a much cheaper stock on 2028 numbers than SanDisk" after SNDK's big move; the LTA disclosure gap (Micron honoring customer confidentiality) may persist but doesn't worry MS. Apple CEO Tim Cook (WSJ) called memory pricing a "100-year flood" (MS "Memory & semicap", 2026-06-22).

## Valuation
- **Historical re-rating arc (Barron's, 2026-06-01 issue):** ~18 months ago MU was "actually the cheapest stock in America relative to earnings" — about **$540** at the start of May [2026]. By the June 1 issue the stock was **over $900** and had just passed a **trillion-dollar market value** (Streetwise/Jack Hough, 2026-06-01). At a recent **$924**, "Rally in Chips" (Barron's/Levine, 2026-06-01) put the stock at **just nine times 2027 EPS** — adjusted EPS seen growing from **$8.29 to over $100 in fiscal 2027**.
- **PEG / forward P·E comps (FactSet via Barron's, 2026-06-01 issue)** — MU screens as the statistical outlier among AI-infrastructure comps on both metrics:

| Ticker | Fwd P/E | PEG |
|---|--:|--:|
| **MU** | **9x** | **0.04** |
| NVDA | 17x | 0.28 |
| AVGO | 23x | 0.37 |
| AMD | 38x | 0.49 |
| TSM | 21x | 0.58 |
| S&P 500 | 20x | 1.01 |

- **Post-F3Q26 read (Barron's, 2026-06-29):** as of late June the stock still traded at **~9x forward earnings** despite the beat-and-raise and 35-of-42 analyst EPS-estimate increases (next-FY consensus EPS $144.27, up from $101.74 a month earlier) — Barron's frames **"another 100% upside"** as available purely from a re-rating toward the S&P multiple, with no further EPS revision required. Read together with the June 22 preview color ("shares surging about 1,150% since the start of 2025... trades at 6.7 times projected 12-month sales, up from a historical average closer to three times" — Barron's/Hough, 2026-06-22), the valuation debate is explicitly framed by Barron's as: cheap on earnings (9x fwd P/E, 0.04 PEG) but expensive on trailing/sales-based multiples relative to MU's own history — consistent with the sell-side's "duration not direction" framing already in Debate/thesis above.
- _Cross-check against sell-side PT methodologies already on this page:_ MS's $1,200 PT / 29.5x through-cycle EPS $35-50, UBS's $1,625 PT / 15x C2029E EPS ~$124-125, and Barclays' pre-print $1,175 / 11x CY27 EPS $106.77 are all **forward/through-cycle-earnings-based**, not sales-based — consistent with the Barron's PEG framework's argument that the cheap-look is real once EPS is annualized on the new SCA-supported floor rather than judged on trailing sales multiples.
- **House PT cross-check (Capstone internal, 2026-07-08):** internal DCF cross-check of the UBS $1,625 price target, built as an editable model — a house-side sanity-check on the SCA-floor earnings-power framing above. (Capstone internal, 2026-07-08)
- **Arete raises PT $852 → $1,500 on a P/B re-rate (Nam Hyung Kim, 2026-07-21):** unchanged Buy; the PT lift comes entirely from the **book-multiple 3.5x → 5.0x** (on revised FY27E BVPS **$297.22**), justified by structurally higher/more-stable ROE from the SCA earnings floor — a different valuation anchor than the forward-EPS PTs elsewhere on the page (MS 30x through-cycle EPS $40 → $1,200; UBS 15x C29E EPS → $1,625; TD Cowen $1,600). Arete's **FY27E EPS $175.39** implies <9x FY27E P/E even at $1,500. Arete explicitly frames the bull case as a memory re-rating **toward HDD suppliers' ~30x P/E** (from memory's historical 5-10x) as LTAs make earnings resilient. (Arete Research "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21)

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **F3Q26 print — REPORTED June 24, 2026 (massive beat-and-raise).** Actuals $41.5B rev / 84.9% GM, F4Q26 guide $50B / ~86% / $31 EPS, 16 SCAs disclosed — see Current state. Next print: F4Q26 (August). Watch SCA roster expansion toward the >50%-of-revenue target, HBM4 ramp, and the capital-return launch post-Dec 2026 CHIPS Act anniversary.
- HBM4 volume ramp in CQ2 2026 for NVIDIA Vera Rubin; HBM4E (custom base die) into CY27. Watch MU's HBM4 base-die redesign / qual progress vs Samsung-Hynix (Jefferies/Inspectrum, 2025-12-17).
- Additional SCA signings / disclosure of structure (floor pricing, cancellation terms) — the re-rating catalyst.
- Capacity: Idaho fab 1 first wafers mid-CY27 (pulled in); Idaho 2 by 2028; New York groundbreaking early CY26; Singapore HBM packaging contributing CY27, NAND fab CY28.
- NAND shortage inflection from 2H26 (MS, 2025-09-10) — eSSD/NL SSD displacement of HDD as a CY26-27 pricing catalyst for the NAND book.
- EUV / tool availability as a 2028 supply governor (JPM: ASML ~90 tools end-CY27 insufficient — Samsung/Hynix each need ~20-25, Micron ~10-15, TSMC already ~40; Micron only recently adopting EUV at 1γ, with EUV reserved for new fabs post-Idaho (Idaho ph.2 / Hiroshima / New York), and if MU can't get EUV by 2028 it would prioritize 1γ for HBM — JPM "Memory", 2026-05-28). UBS frames the same governor more benignly: ASML not a bottleneck until ~110 physical slots (~$225B WFE); ASML is getting the same 8-10-quarter visibility as US toolmakers, so supply is not the limiter (UBS "Arcuri call", 2026-06-15).
- **DRAM price-fixing class action** — a 17-plaintiff class action filed in N.D. Cal. (reported 2026-06-30) alleges Samsung/SK Hynix/Micron conspired to keep DRAM artificially scarce to inflate prices; a legal/headline overhang to monitor, though early-stage (see Intra-quarter log).
- **SCA/LTA roster + lock-through-2030 milestone.** 16 take-or-pay contracts signed, ~40% of revenue once fully executed, terms locked through 2030 (Barron's/Ackerman, 2026-06-29) — watch for the roster to keep growing toward management's >50%-of-revenue target, and for any contract disclosure (price/terms) that lets the market underwrite the "structural shift" thesis with real data rather than characterization language ("well above prior peak"). This is also the single largest debate item on the page right now — see Debate/thesis LTA structural-shift bullet.
- **Next print — F4Q26 (August 2026).** Guided $50B rev / ~86% GM / $31 EPS; 35 of 42 FactSet-tracked analysts already raised EPS post-F3Q26, next-FY consensus EPS $144.27 (up from $101.74 a month prior) — a high bar into the print (Barron's, 2026-06-29).
- **Citi "Upside 90-Day Catalyst Watch" — expires 04 Oct 2026, catalyst = earnings** (Citi / Atif Malik, 2026-07-06). Citi lifted its DRAM ASP QoQ track to +44%/+20%/+13% for 2Q/3Q/4Q26E (from +37%/+13%/+11%) on AI-CPU demand, sees upside to MU's non-contracted ~60% of sales, and has Aug-quarter sales/EPS above Street — the Aug print is the explicit catalyst.
- **CXMT STAR Market IPO listing ~2026-07-16** (China's biggest 2026 IPO; no daily price limit for the first 5 trading days). Competitive-supply milestone to watch: CXMT is guided to exit 2026 with ~60-80k wpm of D1a capacity (litho-tool-gated) and is ~3yr behind on process / 3-4yr on HBM, so near-term it remains a low-end DRAM overhang, but the IPO funds 17nm/DDR5/HBM ambitions (@jukan05 / @zephyr_z9, 2026-07-14; DIGITIMES/BofA prior). Watch the funded capacity ramp as the standing long-dated China-oversupply timing signal.

## Risks
- ASP volatility / cyclicality — 10-K flags "significant volatility in average selling prices" and cash-flow/operating-result volatility that can pressure the credit rating (FY25 10-K).
- Commodity normalization — capacity from Micron/SK/Samsung new fabs landing ~CY27-28 could break the price structure (ex-VP, 2026-06-10).
- Cost-structure disadvantage vs Samsung (geographically dispersed footprint); roughly at SK Hynix (ex-VP, 2026-06-10).
- HBM4 base-die execution — MU redesigning the base die for speed parity and dependent on TSMC/IP partners for advanced logic; Samsung's in-house foundry is an edge (Jefferies/Inspectrum 2025-12-17; SemiAnalysis 2025-08-12).
- **HBM4 pin-speed / qualification risk — sharper version of the above.** SemiAnalysis is explicitly "skeptical of execution" on MU's HBM4 given samples showing much lower pin speeds than Samsung/SK Hynix, and forecasts ~0% MU share of Rubin HBM supply in the first 12 months (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06, p.29) — a more bearish read than the "redesigning for parity" framing elsewhere on this page; watch for qualification updates that resolve this either way. Related capacity risk: the A3 (Idaho) and newly acquired P5 Tongluo fabs are not expected to contribute meaningful output until mid/2H-2027 at the earliest (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania," 2026-02-06, p.23,30), so near-term HBM capacity upside is capped regardless of demand.
- Geopolitics/regulation — export restrictions, tariffs, trade measures; China DRAM substitution via CXMT gaining low-end LPDDR share (10-K; JPM 2026-05-28).
- **China DRAM capacity build-out accelerating beyond CXMT — new long-dated supply overhang.** China's government is working with state-run **Huawei + state-invested SwaySure** to build a **Shenzhen DRAM complex of 140,000 wpm (12-inch), 28nm DRAM**, with ex-TSMC fab director **Steve Liu hired as CEO** and the ex-president of **Elpida** (which Micron acquired) as head strategist; supply-chain reports describe SwaySure as a Huawei "shadow fab" (alongside PXW/PXX) (@dnystedt, 2026-07-15). Sizing the aggregate: @zephyr_z9's 2030-vs-2026 capacity work (via @EugeneNg, 2026-07-15) has **total global DRAM capacity >2x (+128%) by 2030**, the big-3 (Samsung/SK Hynix/Micron) **~2x (+92%)**, **CXMT ~3x (+171%)** and **all of China ~4x (+303%)** with CXMT/YMTC/SwaySure/JHICC — i.e. China grows off a low base far faster than the incumbents, feeding the bear's eventual-oversupply timing debate (low-end/28nm today, not a near-term server/HBM threat). Fresh Korean capacity adds are also landing (Samsung new Giheung DRAM fab ~100k wpm; SK Hynix accelerating its Yongin "Y1" fab, initial ~20k wpm equipment orders — @jukan05, 2026-07-14) — years out, but incremental supply nonetheless.
- **De-spec / content destruction — the under-modelled offset to the price melt-up (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-21).** Distinct from demand elasticity in consumer: the accelerator platforms themselves are cutting DRAM attach *because* memory is scarce and expensive. On AMD's MI455X, the **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier has been removed outright** and DDR5 de-spec'd to a far lower RDIMM content vs an original 2025 BOM of **138TB of DRAM per platform across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM** ("much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs" — SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). The same pattern in the custom parts is a direct HBM-content cut: **Meta's custom MI450/MI455 halves the compute silicon and cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package, and downgrades the HBM4 from 12-Hi to 8-Hi** — at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program SemiAnalysis argues "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin," so the HBM-content read is **doubly negative: fewer stacks per package on a program that may itself shrink** (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21; same conclusion restated 2026-07-24). The risk to Micron is not the DRAM price deck — it is **bits per accelerator**, which the Street generally holds flat or growing. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
  - **Management's direct answer — and it concedes the fact while contesting the cause (Micron IR call · Satish, 2026-07-27).** Asked whether new systems could require less memory by 2028, Satish conceded the observable: "**customers are actually reducing the rate of content growth for memory versus their prior plans, because there is just not enough supply available**" — server units are growing faster than per-server memory, "so the ratio decreases, so the content is expected to grow at a slower rate compared to what customers were previously saying **because of the supply constraint**." But he frames it as a **supply artifact, not a real optimization**: "it's… more a supply related issue **as opposed to an optimization that truly reduces the amount of memory you need per unit of hardware**." On genuine efficiency work (sparse attention, compression, mixture-of-experts) his answer is Jevons — cheaper tokens drive more users *and* longer context/more aggressive agentic workflows, "a net positive in terms of overall demand" — supported by **two Micron white papers published in the last few months (one the prior week)** showing that more LPDDR content raises token throughput and that more HBM4 content or bandwidth does the same. **Why this matters for the bear case:** SemiAnalysis's de-spec evidence and management's account are *not contradictory* — both say bits per unit are being cut today. They differ only on whether the cut **reverses when supply arrives** (management) or **persists as a re-architected BOM** (SemiAnalysis). That is now the single testable question on the content axis, and neither side has settled it. (Micron IR call · Satish, 2026-07-27)
- **Antitrust / price-fixing litigation** — the 06-30 N.D. Cal. class action (17 plaintiffs) is a new legal-risk item on the "big three colluding to keep DRAM scarce" narrative (media report via @dnystedt, 2026-06-30).
- HBM share/competition — SK Hynix leadership (NVIDIA multiyear tech partnership), Samsung HBM4 qualifying and ~20% share rising (Jefferies/Inspectrum 2025-12-17); customers dual-source by design.
- Demand elasticity in price-sensitive PC/smartphone/consumer segments at elevated ASPs (Mehrotra, 2025-12-17).

_**Síntese do quarter — opened 2026-08-03 (first Síntese line on this page; the table above is the audit trail):** the intra-quarter flow has confirmed management's price and floor argument and left exactly one thing unsettled — **bits per accelerator**. On price the window is one-directional: DC prices +25%+ q/q (MS 07-27), July DRAM contract +30-50% q/q (BofA 08-02), and now **HBM4 re-marked to $30-32/GB from $17-18/GB HBM3-class (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur, hosted by Jefferies, 2026-08-03 — ⚠ the transcript itself says "in 2026" in one place and "in 2027" in another; the ambiguity is recorded, not resolved)**. On content the same 08-03 call cuts both ways and that is the whole debate: **the despec MECHANISM is rejected where it would have to happen** — ODM procurement desks call a memory-module cut "unfeasible for AI server," see **no 2027 spec change "including the Google, AWS,"** and are **raising purchasing capex against a $7-8m Rubin rack vs $3-3.5m for a GB200-class rack** — while **the SKU MIX moves against content anyway**, since the >1,024GB four-compute-die Rubin Ultra is cancelled and the surviving two-die part carries **~384GB** (B200 ~192GB / B300 ~288GB). **For MU specifically the unresolved leg is share, not price:** Fubon sees **only a small volume of Micron (and Samsung) HBM on Rubin, with SK Hynix HBM4 the bigger-volume ramp from late-Aug/Sep** — early qualification without volume, which is closer to SemiAnalysis's ~0%-of-first-12-months model than to this page's ~21-22% 2026E bit-share mark. Add one supply-side execution flag that is partly the memory industry's own: **"the HBM base die issue"** is one of the two named causes of the **~two-month Rubin production push-out**. Net: **the earnings floor is intact and getting stronger; the open question is how many HBM bits per package MU gets to sell into it, and on Rubin the answer is currently "fewer than SK Hynix."** ✓ on price · ⚠ on content and share._

| 08-04 | **Invest Like the Best podcast · Gavin Baker (Atreides) — the LTA GAME THEORY, from the buy side** | supply | **bull** | ⚠️ *Informal podcast commentary (~2026-08-04), not verified financials — but it is the most explicit answer yet to the "buyers walk the LTAs in an oversupply" bear that [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) has carried since the 07-31/08-02 LTA-mechanics disclosures.* **The setup: memory is "the axis around which everything is revolving" — "the more memory you put with flops for a given unit of compute, the more tokens you get out… the single most important thing you could do to increase token output per unit of compute," which is why "there's been no elasticity" to memory pricing so far.** **The game theory: four buyers matter at scale ([[AMZN]]/Trainium, [[GOOG]]/TPU, [[AMD]], and [[NVDA]] "much bigger than everybody else combined"). Play it forward to 2027-28: "if you break your LTA and then in the next two or three years FOR ANY REASON leverage shifts back to the memory guys, YOU'RE OUT OF BUSINESS… they say 'fine, you broke the price agreement, we're going to break the volume agreement and give the volume to your competitor.' You just lost share." Market shares in accelerators "for the next several years are going to be determined by supply-chain allocations and what you have pre-purchased" — so breaking an LTA to chase price risks the franchise, and "the game theory almost even holds in a severe oversupply situation."** **The regime change vs the last cycle, named: [[AAPL]] — "going back three, four, five years… they're overwhelmingly the largest purchaser, they know they can do whatever they want with no consequences — even if they super screw Hynix, Micron will of course take them. This is just different: you have at least four players plus all the startups."** **And the capital-structure suggestion: as Hynix CEO he would "do the exact same thing Nvidia is doing right now" — participate in the GPU credit wrapper, put up some cash, and take a ROYALTY / REVENUE SHARE on ongoing compute revenues ("I'm sure our friends at Blackstone and Apollo are suggesting some variant of this to the memory companies") — "almost a logical extension of the LTAs, where they're trading upside for durability."** Cycle color: DRAM spot "this month" is on his list of accelerating metrics; "everybody is citing these LTAs — everything's in a shortage right now." He concedes the market's shift from short-term price capture to LTAs is "a transition in the market that I got wrong." (Invest Like the Best · Gavin Baker, Atreides, ~2026-08-04) [Source](../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-08-04_GavinBaker_ILTB-AI-repricing.md) |

Arcuri models a very large [[WDC]] beat — FQ1 revenue/EPS $4.4B/$4.66 against Street $4.0B/$3.73 — and STILL rates it Neutral, because he holds "a positive CYCLICAL view… and a NEGATIVE STRUCTURAL view relative to the current peaky multiples," arguing customers "will 2X ORDER HDD CAPACITY until demand starts to flatten." He then points the exposure at Micron instead. So the preference is not "memory is good"; it is that in his framework HBM taking capacity from conventional DRAM is a STRUCTURAL supply constraint whereas HDD order books are potentially inflated by duplicate ordering. Note he criticises the market for taking a structural view on HDD "but NOT MEMORY (where HBM continues to take capacity away from traditional DRAM)" — i.e. his complaint is that memory is under-credited for exactly the durability HDD is being over-credited for.** **The tape context makes the call contrarian rather than momentum-chasing: MU is −28% m/m, against [[SKHYNIX]] −34% and [[SAMSUNG]] −22%, with UBS noting "the memory cycle debates remain AT THE TIP OF THE SPEAR."** **Positioning: UBS's desk has MU FIRST in the Semis long-biased list; Susquehanna (Hosseini) carries MU Positive at $823.03** (2026-08-01). ⚠️ **The unresolved counter, which the same desk states rather than hides: "several investors I spoke with are STRUGGLING WITH A FRAMEWORK WHERE SEMI STOCKS CAN CONTINUE TO RALLY WHEN/IF MEMORY PRICES DECLINE even though it would be good for the hyperscalers and the related AI supply chain." | 07-31 | **BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya — Global Memory Tech weekly** | supply | **bull** | **★ The LTA mechanics, and they invert the common bear reading of high contract coverage. "LTA does not fix memory chip price… lot of room to raise contract price every quarter but there is LIMIT FOR PRICE CUT." Specifically: Samsung said 60-70% OF ITS MEMORY SALES WILL BE UNDER LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% down q/q against price INCREASES broadly open at 10-20%+. And on tenor: Samsung's LTAs (mainly for US Big Tech) are mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING, so a 6th year can be added right after or before the 1st year completes.** ⚠️ **So LTAs here are not price locks — they are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE on a rolling tenor. High LTA coverage is therefore bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of how rising contract coverage is usually read at this point in a cycle.** **Spot: sentiment positive into August on three drivers — (1) chip order increases for inventory restocking; (2) many Tier-2 OEMs resuming smartphone/PC launches for Sept/4Q DESPITE high memory cost; (3) China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming out — only 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn, with lean channel inventories. ⚠️ Note the DRAM/NAND divergence in the confirmed July contract prints: TrendForce July DRAM contract price UP LOW-TEENS % M/M = 30-50% Q/Q, but FLAT FOR NAND.** **On China supply, the reason it is not showing up in spot: "minimal impact from China local chipmakers' capacity expansion — China-branded memory NOT COMMONLY TRADED IN THE SPOT MARKET; even Korea/US/Japan chips not much available for white-box OEMs."** Adjacent confirmation that the AI-server pull is broad: **SEMCO and LG Innotek both guided bullishly on AI MLCC and FC-BGA, with 2Q OP more than doubling y/y, stronger LTA orders, LARGE PREPAYMENTS funding new fab construction, and contract price increases through 2H and 2027 — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** |

Kioxia: "we are on track toward our 50% LTA VOLUME COVERAGE FOR CY28 with key customers, providing long-term revenue visibility" — and its Jun-Q ASP was +70% Q/Q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on only LOW-SINGLE-DIGIT BIT GROWTH. Kioxia guides CY26 bit growth high-teens % ("in line with Samsung guidance") and, for CY27, "DEMAND ANTICIPATED TO EXCEED SUPPLY."** Samsung's own call: **bullish memory guidance on high margin, ASP strength, MORE LTAs WITH BIG TECH, HBM4 ramp, and an "ONGOING DRAM SUPPLY SHORTAGE DUE TO AI, EVEN WITH NEW CAPACITY EXPANSION THROUGH 2027-2028"; DRAM OPM ~80% and NAND OPM ~70% in 2Q; DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q** (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The strategic detail worth carrying: Samsung is explicitly trading price for content — ASP guided flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs and "chipmakers' strategic control (e.g., decent price cut per AI server content increase)," with annual OP still rising to W500tn+ on volume and yield. That is a share/volume strategy, not price maximisation — and it caps how far ASP upside can run even in shortage.** Demand mix from Kioxia: **agentic AI "a primary growth catalyst," server NAND benefiting — but smartphone NAND demand FLAT vs CY25 and PC NAND a SLIGHT DECLINE on higher BOM costs.** The cycle is being carried entirely by datacenter. |

So at most like 300K." DRAM share framing: Samsung "around 35 or 38%," SK hynix "like 28%." His view is that in the current market scale IS the competitive variable — "each strengths comes from the volume, how much they can produce" — with Micron's specific edges named as LOW-POWER DRAM and AUTOMOTIVE ("automotive demand for DRAM is high… demand for memory in automotive driving is increasing"), plus high-capacity enterprise SSD alongside the others.** ⚠️ **Useful as a sanity check on any Micron HBM-share model: on a ~300k wpm base, share gains have to come from mix and yield, not from out-building the Koreans.** 🔴 **(2) A BEAR-SIDE HBM PRICE CEILING, and it is the low end of everything this page carries: [[NVDA]] "adjusted 20% increase" on HBM in 1Q, and against the widely-modelled doubling for next year ("HBM4 plus currently around 550, will be doubled next year, like 1,200 or 1,400") the expert expects only "between 20 to 30%," because Nvidia "keep like 75% their profit [margin] for [the] overall GPU system. So they do not increase the BOM, the component cost, the HBM cost very much."** ⚠️ **The mechanism is the CUSTOMER'S margin policy as the constraint on supplier pricing — a different bear from the supply-side ones on this page, and it bears directly on the 2027 HBM ASP line in every model here. Set against SemiAnalysis's "significant increase… to match commodity-DRAM margins" and the 50-100% bull range; neither retracted.** **(3) ON LTAs, the expert credits Micron with going first and going widest: "Micron maybe one or two months ago, they FIRST announced the LTAs… they said 16 entities. That's so huge" — consistent with the "$100bn minimum floor across 14 of 16 SCAs" framework already on this page, from an outside observer.** **(4) SUPPLY-SIDE MARKET, quantified: supply meets only "60% to 70%" of demand, so "whatever they produce, they buy… they do not differentiate from these companies" — an argument that share and product differentiation simply do not bind while the shortage lasts, and that they start binding the moment it ends.** (BTG Pactual-hosted expert call · Hongkyo Jung, former SK Hynix executive, "Beyond the Earnings: What SK Hynix & Samsung Tell Us About the Memory Cycle", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_30_btg_former_hynix_expert_30_07_26.html) |

| 07-28 | Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Joe Moore (+ Sean on Asia memory) | valuation | **mixed** | **The buy-the-dip case stated as a price level rather than a view, plus the cleanest available explanation of *why* the stock keeps having these drawdowns.** Joe Moore's rank order across his coverage is **NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK** — "in my coverage those are the top four… there's nothing else that's wildly compelling." His framing of the pattern is explicitly repetitive: the April selloff came from PC/module-maker inventory plus second-derivative pricing ("all the boxes that you check of what you worry about for DRAM was all in evidence"), then "over the course of the quarter it became clear that the real driving force is AI… numbers were good and stocks went up. I feel like what we're seeing is a lot of **deja vu**. This is the same exact thing and I think this may be with us for a while." He accepts the deceleration rather than disputing it — **prices will be up less in Q3 than Q2, exactly as they were up less in Q2 than Q1: "It has to happen, the numbers are big… just the law of big numbers"** — and draws the tactical conclusion this page should hold onto: **"you're not positive on Micron at 1200, you're positive at 800, because I think you're going to get those kinds of selloffs… I'm going to continue to look to buy on these selloffs rather than be the most enthusiastic guy at the peak."** He acknowledges real demand destruction in PCs and smartphones but starts from "the AI guys aren't close to being fulfilled and none of that has changed." Data: **the DRAM market "has been consistently pretty good"**; NAND, described as "wobbly" a month ago, "has strengthened materially" in the last 30 days (see [[SNDK]]). **Sean's Asia section supplies the sector-level counterweight and the mechanism behind the selloff** — his 07-06 "memory changing tides" three-prong debate (AI spend robust; the LTA re-rating hasn't happened and is an *upside* story; the memory cycle) resolves by elimination onto **the cycle: y/y DRAM pricing likely peaks in 3Q, with 3Q hikes of ~15-20% and 4Q needing +60-70% sequential just to sustain the second derivative — "a bit tough to achieve," and "the market is actually pricing that in at this stage."** Supply is also creeping: **TrendForce DRAM bit growth revised from 25% to ~31% this year and ~24% next**; **NAND from high-teens to 20-25% next year** (YMTC adding a third line, **50,000 wafers**). And TrendForce inventory has ticked up since 2Q — **the first time since 4Q last year — but at the module makers, not the producers**. On capital return, directly relevant to this page's standing CHIPS-Act catalyst: **"for Micron, I think they'll have to wait probably till year end given these restrictions with the Chips Act"** — corroborating the post-Dec-2026 buyback timing already logged. On CXMT, Sean sizes the debut at a **US$492bn close = the 25th most valuable company in the world**, "quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix," and puts the risk in the outer years — "probably doesn't impact this cycle, maybe in the outer years." (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Joe Moore, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html) |
| 07-27 | **Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai — "Global Memory, NVIDIA & Broadcom: Quick thoughts on strategic partnerships" (PRIMARY PDF, 16pp; ingested 2026-08-11)** | valuation | **bull** | **A partnership note, not a MU note — MU appears in the ticker table and in the ratings rationale, and the page should weight it accordingly. But the marks are net-new here and the rationale is the sharpest one-line statement of the China-DRAM debate the wiki has.** **RATING: Outperform, PT $1,300** (px $920.95 at the 24-Jul close, +708% relative performance) — valuation stated as **7.7x forward 5Q-8Q EPS of US$169.8**, the highest multiple Bernstein applies to any memory name in the note (vs 6.2x for [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]], 4.1x for [[KIOXIA]]). **EPS $8.29 TTM / $67.39 (2026E) / $158.99 (2027E) = 111.1x / 13.7x / 5.8x adjusted P/E.** PT history in the note: **$510 (18-Mar-2026) → $1,300 (22-Jun-2026)**. 🔴 **THE RATIONALE, and it is a product-level discriminator rather than a house view on memory: "We remain constructive on memory & find the recent pullback a good entry point. We find MEMORY IS MORE IMPORTANT TO AI THAN LOGIC SEMICONDUCTORS and are thus positive on demand. We however worry about the long-term threat from China in NAND and thus rate KIOXIA Underperform. THE THREAT IS MUCH LOWER IN DRAM AS CHINA EVENTUALLY WILL FIND IT DIFFICULT TO COMPETE IN DRAM MARKET WITHOUT EUV. We thus rate Samsung, SK hynix and Micron Outperform."** ⚠️ **Two things to take from that for this page. (1) It is a LITHO-ACCESS argument, not a capacity argument — which is a direct challenge to how the CXMT threat is measured on this page and on [[SAMSUNG]]'s: the UBS 240K→292K→382K→466K wpm ramp and JPM's 16% capacity / 11% bit share by 28E both measure the input Bernstein says will not decide the outcome. Neither side is netted; the disagreement is about the metric, not the number. (2) The same sentence is why Bernstein is Outperform on all three DRAM names and Underperform on the one NAND-pure name — i.e. MU's rating here is a DRAM-franchise rating, and its NAND exposure is being implicitly discounted rather than argued.** **Industry frame: consensus memory revenue ~US$0.9T (CY26) → US$1.3T (CY27) → US$1.3T (CY28), split across Samsung Memory / SK hynix / Micron / SanDisk / KIOXIA / CXMT / YMTC, with CXMT and YMTC on Bernstein's own estimates (Exhibit 3) — note the FLATNESS from '27 to '28, which is a consensus artefact worth attacking rather than a Bernstein forecast.** **Risks as stated: "an earlier end of favorable pricing environment, which can be a result of weaker demand or higher supply," investor sentiment/valuation, and China's memory progress especially in NAND.** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai / Alrick Shaw / Arpad von Nemes / Eva Zhang, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260726_Bernstein_NVDA_Global_Memory-_NVIDIA_-_Broadcom-_Quick_thoughts_on_stra.html) |
| 07-27 | **Micron IR call · Satish** (2nd call hosted for Brazilian institutional investors) | guidance | **bull** | 🔴 **The most complete SCA disclosure management has given, and the single most useful line in it is a warning about a number the Street is already using.** **The RPO is a floor, not a forecast:** the **~$100bn RPO** across the **14 of 16 contracts that carry a pricing framework** is calculated as **contract volume × the FLOOR price**, and Satish volunteers the caution — "the pricing is at the **ceiling** right now… **the revenue that we will actually get on the SCAs, we expect will be well above the RPO levels. The RPO is not indicative of the revenue that we will get**." **End-state architecture, net-new in this granularity:** **up to 50%+ of total revenue under SCAs**, of which **~40% carries a pricing framework** (band or fixed) and **~10% is volume-only take-or-pay with price negotiated quarterly**, leaving **~50% market-priced**; today's framework tranche is "**in the low 20%**" vs the **25% of revenue across 16 contracts** disclosed at earnings. *This reconciles the page's ">50% of revenue" and "~40% of revenue" figures as one plan on two tranches, not two estimates.* **All SCAs carry take-or-pay volume commitments.** **Band mechanics:** **ceiling ≈ the market price in the June calendar quarter**, "roughly consistent with the type of pricing that we were getting in our results and guidance" — i.e. already inside the **86% Aug-quarter GM guide** (up from ~85% the prior quarter); **floor GM "well above any quarterly cyclical peak gross margin," with the prior peak named as fiscal 2018 "in the low 60% range" — and explicitly "we haven't quantified that level."** Bands run the full contract term. **Tightness walked forward again:** management framing has gone **2026 → 2027 → "beyond calendar 27 and even in 28"**, and "**we actually do not see a line of sight to when the supply demand balance even comes back into balance**." **China exposure halved: high-20s% two years ago → low-double-digit % today**, on Chinese-government restrictions on Chinese DC buyers plus MU's own choice to skip NAND mobile; auto/robotics customers remain. **Tech moat vs CXMT (answered on CXMT's debut day): ~3 nodes ahead; 1-gamma (EUV) >half of DRAM production around this time of year; 1-delta next with more EUV; ~65,000 patents over ~50 years** and a track record of defending them. **Capital: expects to return 100% of excess cash to shareholders over time.** **The one concession worth marking as a bear datapoint:** customers **are** cutting the *rate* of memory content growth versus prior plans — but Satish frames it as a supply artifact, "not… an optimization that truly reduces the amount of memory you need per unit of hardware" (see the de-spec bullet in Risks). (Micron IR call · Satish, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_27_mu_ir_call_27_jul_26.html) |

| 06-29 | Jefferies/Fubon · Conor O'Mara | guidance | bull | **Micron IR call (Satya Kumar) — SCA mechanics and business model transformation.** Full detail from Fubon-hosted call with Micron's head of IR. **SCA scope:** 16 signed; 20% of DRAM volume + 33% of NAND volume = **25% of total Micron sales**; discussions ongoing → target **up to 50% of sales**. **Financial commitments:** $22B total — **$18B cash deposits** on the balance sheet (unrestricted, interest-bearing, returned in 2H of contract term; goes into financing CF, not operating CF — not escrow) + **$4B letters of credit**. **Pricing — 3 tiers:** (1) ceiling = CYQ2 market price (= the disclosed floor on buyer cost); (2) floor = margins **"well above prior peak cycle"** (not a specific $/GB number; floor is high — confirmed above any prior-cycle peak GM); (3) fixed pricing for smaller-volume contracts; 2 of 16 have floating pricing. Long-run target: **~40% of Micron sales** at band or fixed pricing. **Why different from prior LTAs:** On supply — Moore's Law slowing makes bits harder to add; HBM trade ratio reduces supply; product complexity rising → new fabs genuinely harder to build. On demand — AI shifted demand growth from mid-teens to **20s%**; supply-constrained; inference makes memory the **cost-per-token bottleneck** (not the GPU). On product — deep collaboration with model companies like **Anthropic**; memory roadmap strategically embedded in customer planning. **Capex:** FY27 above **mid-$40B** (FY26 ~$37B); **>50% for facilities**. **CY26 bit shipments:** DRAM **low-20s%** (including HBM); NAND **~20%**. **Demand:** remains above supply through 2027 and into 2028; agentic AI → larger context windows + KV Cache → structural memory demand step-up. **HBM vs. conventional:** not a simple switch — customers need matched sets; MU working closely on mix but it is not "just flip the dial." **NAND new apps:** Storage NEXT (NVIDIA, requires **50–100M IOPS** vs 5M standard) + KV Cache (shorter tenure; endurance-focused — TLC/QLC upside; potentially fewer bits/cell). (Jefferies/Fubon · Conor O'Mara, 2026-06-29) |

| 07-24 | SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026" | produto | **mixed** | **The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off, the 2027 HBM-price call, and the de-spec that offsets it — all in one note, read off the buyer's BOM.** (1) **Pin speed:** AMD's MI455X carries **12 HBM4 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s** — a near-tie despite AMD's **50% wider bus**, because AMD runs **7.6 Gbps** pin speed vs Nvidia's **10.7 Gbps (40% faster)**. Nvidia "aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X." That "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but as of today these issues have been finally resolved" — and **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD**. AMD + Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. Net for MU: a **two-tier HBM4 market by construction** (easy 7.6 Gbps AMD bin vs premium 10.7 Gbps Nvidia bin) — a share/mix question, not a volume one, and the axis on which the page's standing MU pin-speed skepticism resolves. SemiAnalysis does **not** say who wins the top bin. (2) **Bull — 2027 pricing:** expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," strong enough that AMD "can't pass the full memory increase on to its customers" → **gross-margin drag on the MI455X package**; "HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." (3) **Bear — de-spec:** original 2025 MI455X BOM carried **138TB of DRAM (LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM)**; current estimate has **LPDDR5X removed entirely** (the up-to-**1TB** per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier is gone) plus a significant DDR5 RDIMM de-spec — "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" The mechanism behind MS/Moore's same-day "LTAs and de-speccing flatten amplitude, extend duration" line. Demand supported by North-American CSP capex, new Intel/AMD CPU platforms and agentic-AI commercialisation, with higher per-box HBM and SOCAMM content; consumer (phones, notebooks) shipments expected to decline on higher pricing. **Sufficiency gap ~-1% to -2% in 2026, widening in 2027.** NAND — 2027 sees accelerated high-layer-count adoption and new fab output, so **bit supply growth exceeds the prior year and the market turns looser in 2H27 with prices at risk of correction**; demand held up by AI servers and high-capacity QLC eSSD. Caveat flagged: expensive memory raises memory's share of CSP capex, and whether that affects procurement is still open |
| 07-30 | @barronsonline | valuation | bear | "**Micron Stock Just Can't Get Going—Even After Samsung's 1,800% Profit Surge**" — the standing complaint that the memory tape has decoupled from memory fundamentals. Cross-check on valuation: **MU and SNDK around ~5x FY2 earnings vs NVDA ~15x** post the Korea selloff (@The_AI_Investor, 2026-07-30) |
| 07-30 | Sinocism · Bill Bishop | competicao | bear | **CXMT is now a live political fact, not a 2027 risk.** "A Memory-Maker Makes History" — CXMT's IPO **rose 466% on debut to become No.1 in the China stock market** (WSJ). US lawmakers are seeking a national-security probe ("ticking time bomb," NY Post) and **Apple is actively lobbying to relax restrictions on US companies working with PRC memory firms** — WSJ headline: "Trump Caught Between Apple and Micron in Fight Over Chinese Chips." SemiAnalysis: "China's CXMT Is Set to Challenge DRAM Incumbents." _Counterpoint from the same day: the BTG former-SK-hynix expert argues Chinese makers are effectively blocked from advanced DRAM/HBM by equipment export controls (see SKHYNIX page)._ |

**Quarter synthesis:** post-F3Q26 print, the street has moved to uniformly bullish and the narrative has shifted from cycle direction to earnings durability / FCF inflection. The SCA framework ($100B minimum floor across 14 of 16 SCAs) is the structural re-rating catalyst; SFG frames the FY27 path as "meaningful FCF generation" (~$160 annualized EPS). The Anthropic supply agreement adds a high-profile demand anchor. New from 06-27/28: mobile margin data (~87% GM, +15× GP/unit YoY) confirms the pricing power magnitude; Apple's YMTC/CXMT approval request signals customers actively seeking alternatives — bull on near-term pricing power, medium-term competitive flag. Gavin Baker + Elon Musk (Terafab focus on DRAM) corroborate the "DRAM is the AI inference bottleneck" thesis independently. New 06-29: UBS (Arcuri) puts hard numbers on the SCA economics — "well above prior peak" ≈ 70-75% GM, an implied ~$5/GB SCA floor against a ~$10.50/GB ceiling, and a floor that doesn't bind until DDR falls ~75-80% from a high-teens peak (not modeled before Aug-2029) — reinforcing the earnings-durability case while underscoring that the SCAs protect margin floors, not current peak pricing. JPM Jay Kwon adds the Korea $880B macro backdrop: government-backed Yongyin pull-forward (7–12 yrs), and CXMT IPO prospectus confirming supply insufficient for even domestic demand (neutralizes near-term China oversupply fear). **Jefferies/Fubon Micron IR call (Satya Kumar)** provides the most complete SCA mechanics picture to date: $18B cash deposits (unrestricted, interest-bearing), $4B in LCs; pricing floor is "well above any prior-cycle peak GM"; target 40-50% of sales under SCA over time; FY27 capex above mid-$40B; demand above supply through 2028; inference KV Cache makes memory the cost-per-token bottleneck — not the GPU. The "why different this time" framework (supply harder to add, demand structurally shifted, product strategically embedded) is now on the record from IR, making the SCA-driven business model transformation the primary investment thesis. **Also new 06-29:** Barron's Tech Trader ("Micron Breaks the Cycle. Why the Stock Could Still Double") puts a mainstream-financial-press wrapper on the same SCA thesis — 16 take-or-pay contracts, ~40% of revenue once fully executed, locked through 2030 (BNP's Ackerman: "transformative shift... mitigated cyclicality"); the CBO's own concession that "it'll take investors some time to understand all the details" corroborates the disclosure-gap point MS/Moore flagged earlier as "a little mixed." Critically, despite 35-of-42 analysts raising EPS (next-FY consensus $144.27, from $101.74 a month prior), the stock is *still* only ~9x forward earnings — Barron's argues the re-rating (not further EPS revision) is the remaining catalyst, worth "another 100% upside" alone. This is the most explicit statement on the page yet that the LTA/SCA framework is simultaneously the biggest bull catalyst (de-risked cyclicality → higher multiple deserved) and the debate's central unresolved question (a reader comment on the SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania" piece flags the LTA wave as simply "very different from the past" — a structural break the market has no back-tested precedent for, cutting both ways). **New 06-30:** TrendForce revised its (previously most-conservative) DRAM base case *up* — 3Q26 PC DRAM +15-20% QoQ (from +8-13%), server DRAM +13-18% — and reiterated shortage-through-2028 on cleanroom-limited supply, i.e. pricing is running hotter than even the bearish desk modeled; offsetting these bullish datapoints, a 17-plaintiff DRAM price-fixing class action was filed in N.D. Cal. (new legal/headline overhang) and SemiAnalysis flagged CXMT as clearly the No. 4 DRAM player expanding on strong cash flow (low-end competitive creep). Watch: HBM4 qualification progress, capital-return launch post-Dec 2026 CHIPS Act anniversary, Apple/CXMT waiver outcome as a long-run competitive risk monitor, Storage NEXT NAND ramp timeline, the price-fixing suit's docket. **New 06-30/07-01:** Jefferies/O'Mara's Micron IR call adds SOCAMM (196GB/256GB, broadest-portfolio claim) and a new NAND product line — "Super IO" high-IOPS NAND (50-100M IOPS vs 5M today) aimed at KV-cache/vector-DB workloads, directly vs Kioxia — plus QLC-endurance/TLC-for-KV-cache exploration; demand still seen outrunning supply through CY27 into CY28. JPM (Meyers citing Kwon) frames the Apple-Micron pricing dispute as crystallizing market anxiety over DRAM profit-sharing sustainability, alongside the N.D. Cal. price-fixing suit and the early-2000s cartel precedent as the bear's reference case — a capital/legal overhang worth tracking distinct from the fundamentals. Separately, JPM/Kwon's Korea framing steps up materially — **~$3.1tn through 2040** (vs the $880B/10-yr figure logged 06-29), 60-70% to front-end wafer spend, doubling Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years, memory execs citing supply at only ~50% of demand, $12-20tn cumulative Korean-memory revenue opportunity — the two Korea figures are not reconciled to a single plan and both remain logged. On 07-01: Jefferies' Favuzza flags MU as the **#1 single-name driver of both the NDX (26% of 1H26 gain) and SPX (17%) YTD** — a crowding/rotation risk given the -2.9% pre-market move on a broader leader/laggard rotation, not a fundamental deterioration; MS/Moore's Semiconductor Inventory Tracker names MU a preferred memory name even as sector-wide inventory (33 days above historical median) shows no broad restocking cycle yet — i.e. the MU-specific tightness is not yet a sector-wide phenomenon. **07-07:** UBS's SpaceX/SPCX-initiation "Terafab" note is a long-dated competitive-risk read-through for MU — Musk names Micron among Terafab's existing suppliers (wanting faster expansion) while Terafab *explicitly targets memory*; UBS flags it is unclear where Terafab would source memory IP and sees limited incentive for incumbents to license, but a new at-scale memory supplier (if successful) could eventually pressure incumbents — a monitor item consistent with the standing CXMT/ex-VP-Procurement "differentiation is temporary" bear axis, not a near-term thesis-mover. **07-08/07-09:** the flow stayed constructive on pricing/demand into the new week — Bernstein's Memory tracker (Newman & Li, 07-08) shows DRAM & NAND contract prices up mildly MoM with the tracker still pointing to a major 2QCY26 increase (server demand absorbing supply, some consumer demand destruction, growth rates slowing); Goldman's ~$1T 2027 hyperscaler capex with ~40-50% to memory (@ParadisLabs), @QQ_Timmy's ~50%-over-two-quarters move (AI broadening from HBM into general DRAM/NAND) and BofA's HBM $/rack ~quintupling Blackwell→Rubin (via @firstadopter) size the demand pool, and Newman published a Memory-LTA deep dive + structure call (07-09) squarely on the SCA-durability debate. Offsetting: CXMT launched a ~CNY29.5bn (~US$4.1bn) STAR Market IPO (China's biggest 2026 IPO) to fund 17nm/DDR5/HBM and is targeting HBM3 mass production by end-2026 (still in qual, per @zephyr_z9) — the low-end competitive-creep bear now reaching into HBM3. On the tape, @bespokeinvest notes MU has shed nearly a quarter of its value in a week yet still closed above its 50-DMA. **Into the same 07-09 date, TD Cowen (Krish Sankar) sets its July-print bull mark — Buy, PT $1,600** (near the top of the range, just below UBS $1,625) — and **Bernstein (Mark Newman) published "New Memory LTAs part 2 - Case studies from Microchip,"** the clearest historical frame yet on the deposits debate: MU's $18B cash-deposit + $4B LC backing is back-end-weighted (coverage → 75-100% of remaining obligations in the outer years) and gives "meaningful but not infinite" downside protection — better-structured than the old Microchip-PSP / Hemlock-polysilicon LTAs that broke through prior downturns, a direct constructive counter to the "deposits = vendor financing / never tested through a downturn" bear axis. **The debate's two sides are now both Newman/Bernstein, same day:** the separate **"Memory LTA effectiveness" note (07-09)** is the skeptic's quantitative bound on that same thesis — LTA effectiveness is impossible to quantify precisely, the ~$3.33B industry deposit is <1% (~0.6%) of the revenue an LTA book must protect (a "secure" 20% deposit would need ~$1tn no customer has), the Micron–Anthropic *equity-stake* structure is "vendor financing" that doesn't reduce investor risk, and the coverage ceiling is only ~50-67% of the market (PC/smartphone customer-customers transactional per SanDisk's CEO; China prefers local). Net, Bernstein's own framing — "meaningful but not infinite" — is the reconciliation: the deposits are structurally better than legacy LTAs (part 2) yet quantitatively cover only a fraction of full downside (effectiveness note). Separately, **SK Hynix's ADR roadshow (07-06)** had the #1 HBM competitor publicly frame Micron's floor-price SCA structure as one of several viable LTA designs (SK Hynix "focus[es] upon margin" not a floor), and **MS's NVDA NDR note (07-10)** kept MU Overweight while recapping its semis Top-Pick rotation (NVDA→SanDisk→Micron→back to NVDA on value) and flagging that its multi-year-shortage/static-supply memory commentary "could dampen enthusiasm for memory stocks." **New 07-10/07-11:** the supply-tightness narrative got fresh corroboration — SK Hynix's CEO called 2027 "the worst year in the industry's history from a supply perspective" with demand exceeding capacity for a decade (via @jukan05, 07-11), and Nanya's Jun-26 blowout (rev +684% y/y, GM 79.5%, ASP +>500% y/y on ~flat bits; @SKundojjala, 07-10) shows the ASP-driven tightness is broad across the DRAM complex, not MU-specific. On capital, Micron stepped up its US investment plan to **~$250bn through 2035** (Reuters via @C_Barraud & @TrumpDailyPosts, 07-10) and — tied to the 07-09 $3B ecosystem announcement — prepaid **$500mn to GlobalWafers under a 10-yr silicon-wafer LTA**; Nomura (07-12 note) reads the early LTA as a cash-rich/favorable-S/D signal but flags a cost read-through (semi-wafer LTA pricing "could double" off a <1%-of-memory-wafer-cost base = modest silicon-wafer cost inflation inbound for MU). **New 07-13→07-16:** the week split cleanly into (a) a pricing/demand confirmation and (b) a stepped-up China-supply overhang. On pricing — **BofA's 07-13 "DRAM ASPs look better than investor concerns"** pushed back directly on the Korea/Japan memory-stock correction (no earnings miss; conventional-memory ASP already +50-60% QoQ; full **+53%/+21%/+7% QoQ 2Q/3Q/4Q** DRAM-ASP cadence with **>50% of conventional DRAM still off-LTA**, i.e. price upside remaining). The correction's proximate trigger — SK Hynix's 2Q26 print ~8% below consensus — was attributed by KIS/Mirae to HBM-mix + the **LTA ASP-smoothing effect kicking in** (a smoothing artifact, read-through-supportive of MU's own SCA thesis, not demand weakness); PSMC's **+45% July DRAM wafer-start hike** and Ray Wang/SemiAnalysis's "no supply relief until 2H27, undersupply into 2028" corroborate broad DRAM-complex tightness. On supply — the China build-out visibly stepped up: a new **Huawei/SwaySure Shenzhen DRAM complex (140k wpm, 28nm)**, **CXMT's STAR Market IPO listing ~07-16** (exiting 2026 with ~60-80k wpm D1a), and @zephyr_z9's 2030 capacity map (all-China DRAM ~4x/+303% vs big-3 ~2x/+92%), plus fresh Korean adds (Samsung Giheung ~100k wpm; SK Hynix Yongin Y1) — long-dated and low-end today but feeding the bear's eventual-oversupply timing debate, which Barron's captured in its explicit two-sided framing (bull "rising nearly 90%" 07-14 vs bear "Chinese competition about to get fiercer" 07-15). Net: near-term pricing/demand signal remains ✓-confirming; the incremental new-news is on the long-dated China-capacity risk axis, not the current cycle. **07-20 (memory-selloff reframed as entry point):** MS (Joe Moore, "US memory selloff = entry point") reiterates **OW / PT $1,200** (bull $1,650 / bear $675; 30x through-cycle EPS $40) — DC memory prices "up 25%+ in 3q" above MS/3rd-party estimates, MU holds HBM share in CY26, FY Oct26e sales $128,983mn ≈ Street $129,134mn, DRAM in "uncharted territory" with shortage intensifying into 2027/2028 → "buy the selloff" (no PT change — the $1,200/$1,650/$675 set was already on the page). BofA's 07-20 lightning round adds the input-cost nuance (DRAM +60%/3mo vs HBM "not up that much"), and Octahedron's 2Q26 LP call (FUND estimate) frames MU long-but-consensus with LTAs that "basically guarantee volumes and floors" and memory re-rating toward how NVDA/Avago trade on HBM volume+pricing. Supply/ASP signal stays ✓ confirms; no house/BBG estimate superseded. **07-24 (SemiAnalysis Advancing AI 2026) — the run's one genuinely new axis, and it is a *content* axis, not a price axis.** On price the note is confirmatory and unusually well-sourced because it comes from the *buyer's* bill of materials rather than a memory maker: SemiAnalysis expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," strong enough that AMD cannot pass it all through and takes a gross-margin hit on the MI455X package — i.e. HBM pricing power is now modelled as compressing the accelerator vendors, with commodity-DRAM margin as the anchor (the same inversion UBS and BofA already flagged from the memory side). What is new is the offset: the *same* BOM shows an original 2025 MI455X DRAM content of **138TB across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM** collapsing to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM figure with **LPDDR5X removed entirely** — the up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier simply gone on tight supply — and SemiAnalysis's own summary is that "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs." Meta's custom MI450/MI455 is the sharpest instance (HBM **12 stacks → 6**, **12-Hi → 8-Hi**, at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program the same house says will "nuke AMD's volume at Meta"; SemiAnalysis 07-21/07-24). This is the mechanism sitting underneath MS/Joe Moore's same-day "LTAs and de-speccing flatten amplitude but extend duration" framing — and it argues the marginal risk to 2027 memory revenue has moved from *price* to *bits per accelerator*, which most models hold flat. On the supplier-differentiation axis, the HBM4 pin-speed bake-off (AMD 7.6 Gbps / 12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s vs Rubin 10.7 Gbps / 8 stacks / 288GB / 22 TB/s, with Nvidia having pushed above the JEDEC spec deliberately, forcing suppliers to rework HBM4 and requiring "a much higher-quality bin") creates a **two-tier HBM4 market** — the axis on which this page's standing MU pin-speed skepticism (Memory Mania, ~0% Rubin share yr-1) resolves in either direction. SemiAnalysis does not name a bin winner and neither should we. Net: price signal ✓ confirms, content signal ⚠ new; no house/BBG estimate, PT or rating superseded. **07-27/07-28 (Micron IR call + MS memory webcast) — the run's contribution is disclosure quality on the SCA debate and a tactical frame for the drawdown, not a change in direction.** On the SCAs, **Micron IR (Satish, 07-27) closed the page's largest open disclosure gap and did it against the Street's own use of the number: the ~$100bn RPO is contract volume × the FLOOR price, and management said unprompted that actual SCA revenue "will be well above the RPO levels… the RPO is not indicative of the revenue that we will get," because pricing is currently at the CEILING.** Anyone marking MU's SCA book at $100bn is marking it at the contractual minimum. The architecture is now fully specified — **up to 50%+ of revenue under SCAs = ~40% priced-framework + ~10% volume-only take-or-pay + ~50% market**, with the framework tranche "in the low 20%" today against 25% of revenue across 16 contracts at earnings — which **reconciles the ">50%" and "~40%" figures this page has carried side by side as one plan measured on two tranches**. The ceiling is confirmed as ≈ the June-calendar-quarter market price and therefore already embedded in the **86% Aug-quarter GM guide** (up from ~85%); the floor GM is again only *characterised* — "well above" the fiscal-2018 low-60s% peak, "we haven't quantified that level" — so **UBS's ~70-75% GM / ~$5-per-GB back-solve remains an inference, and Bernstein's "meaningful but not infinite" bound remains untested**. Management also walked the tightness horizon out a third time (**2026 → 2027 → "beyond calendar 27 and even in 28"**, with no line of sight to balance) and halved the China exposure story (**high-20s% → low-double-digit %**, part restriction, part MU's own NAND-mobile abstention). The genuinely two-sided item is on **content**: Satish concedes customers are cutting the *rate* of memory content growth versus prior plans, but attributes it to supply rather than optimization — which means the SemiAnalysis de-spec evidence and management's account agree on the fact and disagree only on **whether it reverses when supply arrives**. That is the cleanest statement yet of the open question on the content axis. On the tape, **MS's 07-28 webcast reframes the drawdown as structural rather than informational**: Joe Moore ranks **NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK**, accepts the Q3 deceleration outright ("the law of big numbers"), and reduces the call to a price — **"you're not positive on Micron at 1200, you're positive at 800"** — while Sean's three-prong elimination lands the selloff squarely on **the cycle prong: y/y DRAM pricing peaks in 3Q, with 3Q hikes ~15-20% and 4Q needing +60-70% sequential to sustain the second derivative, which the market "is actually pricing in."** Supply is creeping at the margin too (TrendForce DRAM bits **25% → 31%** this year, **~24%** next; NAND high-teens → **20-25%** next year on YMTC's third line at **50,000 wafers**), and TrendForce inventory ticked up for the first time since 4Q last year — **at module makers, not producers**, which is the distinction that keeps it a channel datapoint rather than a demand one. Sean also corroborates the standing capital-return timing: the **MU buyback "will have to wait probably till year end given these restrictions with the Chips Act."** Net: **✓ confirms on structure and pricing level, ⚠ nuances on the second derivative and on content; no rating, PT or estimate superseded.**

**🆕 08-08→08-10 flow — three houses, one direction (HBM up, NAND noisy, stock oversold):**

| Date | Broker · Analyst | Tema | Viés | What it said |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 08-09 | JPM · Jay Kwon / Harlan Sur / Mio Shikanai ("Global Memory TAM and S&D update") | demanda | bull | FY26E-28E **memory TAM raised 4-8%** vs the May-26 model; **S-D shortage continues, worsening in FY27E** then easing slightly into FY28E; **bid-to-supply still 70-80%** for next year (vs ~90% at the pandemic peak). HBM carries the most revision — longer 8Hi cycle, slower 12Hi ramp, 16Hi adoption delayed — but **2027 HBM pricing revised UP to +42% y/y**. Memory value share of CSP capex to **31%/49% in 2026E/27E** from <10% pre-AI. DRAM WSPM **1.9M (25A-end) → 2.85M (28-end)**, still short — **~300K extra wafers needed for 2028E balance, "probability low."** DRAM ASP **+235% in 26E, then +29%/+7% in 27/28E** under LTA smoothing. LTAs: **prepayment 20-25% of value, volume 50-70%, AI/server tranche at a 30-40% price premium**. Verdict: "3Q26 will likely be an expectation reset quarter, but we remain bullish" — memory shares **-25% in 3Q26TD**; OW MU in the US. |

Frames the recent tape as channel noise: module houses balking at post-June prices (same pattern as late-Feb and early-June, both of which resolved with buyers accepting), while **manufacturers selling to hyperscalers/OEMs realise a different, higher price**. Enterprise demand the underappreciated 2H26/2027 wildcard; **2Tb QLC mass-capacity SSD adoption at an inflection**, 332-layer QLC lifting bit density **>50%**. With the **DRAM Memory ETF -40% from its late-June peak**, calls risk/reward attractive. **Preferred: Micron (HBM share gains against SK Hynix) and SanDisk.** |

_Read-through to the Sinal table: the 08-10 UBS note **partially closes the "bits per accelerator" row** left open in the 08-03 synthesis — the VR300 de-spec is now sized (384GB → 512GB HBM4E, vs 768GB prior) and shown to be **more than offset by unit count** (C27 HBM consumption 58.7 → 61.5B Gb). ✓ on price stays ✓ and strengthens (HBM/DDR premium re-widening, +79% Y/Y blended). ⚠ on content softens to **⚠→✓ on aggregate bits, still ⚠ on MU's share of them** — nothing in this week's flow speaks to the Fubon claim that SK hynix carries the bigger Rubin HBM4 volume from late-Aug/Sep. **New ⚠ on NAND**, which was not a live row before: UBS cut C3Q NAND contract pricing to +20% Q/Q from +28% and MU to +23% from +35%, while SIG argues the NAND softness is channel-level, not manufacturer-level. Síntese unchanged in direction; the NAND leg is the one to watch into the F4Q26 print._

- **2026-08-07 (wiki-ingest, scheduled) — 1 intra-quarter row. Nothing on this page superseded (UBS rates MU Buy, unchanged); the value is that BOTH live bears got answered and the industry revenue forecast was cut anyway.**
  🔴 **BEAR 1 — THE HBM DE-SPEC IS RESOLVED AND INVERTED. UBS: Rubin Ultra goes HBM4 8-Hi then HBM4E 8-Hi, NOT the previously expected HBM4E 12-Hi, "IN RESPONSE TO PERSISTENT TIGHT SUPPLY" — per-GPU content 384GB → 512GB against a 768GB prior estimate. BUT 2027 [[NVDA]] HBM end-consumption was RAISED to 25.1bn Gb (from 24.0bn) "AS GPU UNITS IMPLIED GO UP TO 12.6MIL FROM 11.0MIL." Less memory per die, more dies: a bill of materials rationed by SUPPLY, not softening demand. Industry HBM +91% y/y to 61.5bn Gb in '27; blended HBM ASP +79% y/y (was +66%).**
  🔴 **BEAR 2 — CXMT IS DISMISSED WITH A REUSABLE MECHANISM: 9% bit share in '27 vs 7% in '25; capacity 240K → 292K → 382K WPM through 4Q27; "market expectations are PROBABLY MORE ALIGNED WITH EQUIPMENT DELIVERY VS ACTUAL INSTALLATION/VALIDATION… AND HENCE TOO HIGH"; G5 sub-15nm late '26 versus MU/Samsung/SK Hynix at 1d nm sub-11nm in 2H27, with yields "NOT AT PAR" partly due to equipment export control.**
  ✅ **THE CORE DRAM CASE STRENGTHENS: 2027 DRAM bit demand raised to +40.1% y/y (from +36%) on server DDR, against supply growth of only +20.6% — "a PROLONGED DRAM UNDERSUPPLY CYCLE THROUGH AT LEAST 2Q28," with DRAM explicitly running longer than NAND (2Q28E vs 4Q27E). Volume corroboration from the SIA data: CQ2 memory UNITS +32.2% q/q, "confirming that this cycle is NOT PURELY AN ASP PHENOMENON."**
  ⚠️ **AND THE CUT: UBS lowered '27E memory industry revenue 9% to US$1.61tn ('26E US$0.94tn) because "LTAs ARE REIGNING IN SOME OF THE PRICING UPSIDE INTO THE UPCYCLE," compensated by LTAs being "OPM/ROE ACCRETIVE THROUGH THE CYCLE." Named risk: "MEMORY SPENDING SUSTAINABILITY FOR CUSTOMERS, NOTABLY HYPERSCALERS."** ⚠️ **Jefferies attacks the same trade from the other side the same day: LTAs "EXTENUATE A CYCLE RATHER THAN PREVENT IT"; "DEALS SIGNED AT 80% GROSS MARGIN IMPLY SOFTER ASPs THAN OPEN MARKET, QUESTIONING THE UPSIDE POTENTIAL from historical $300-500 EPS expectations"; and — read off the equipment layer — "[[LRCX]]'s RECORD NAND REVENUE indicates SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY in NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN ANTICIPATED." Both views retained; the LTA question is now the central debate on the memory complex.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW DEMAND-SIDE RISK TO WATCH, filed because it bears directly on the "agentic CPU" leg logged here on 08-06: [[QCOM]] IR says its High Bandwidth Compute "DOESN'T USE HBM — IT USES LPDDR — AND IT ALSO DOESN'T USE CoWoS," with the accelerator die placed under the LPDDR stack via TSVs, claiming 6x the per-watt bandwidth of HBM and commercial sampling mid-2027, and that "QCOM ALREADY HAS THE MEMORY ALLOCATION NEEDED."** ⚠️ **Company claims, unverified — but if the route scales it shifts decode-stage demand from HBM into conventional DRAM, which is a mix effect this page's HBM-led thesis does not currently model.** Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[NVDA]], [[QCOM]], [[SNDK]], [[LRCX]], [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [china-export](themes/china-export.md). - **2026-08-06 — /wiki-ingest (scheduled). 4 new Full-log rows. Nothing superseded — no rating, PT or house estimate changed (DB Buy PT $1,550 reiterated). The value is four net-new MECHANISMS, two of which cut against the bull case.**
  🔴 **THE BEST SINGLE ITEM: DB · Melissa Weathers hosted Micron's Jeremy Werner (SVP/GM Core Data Center) and Satya Kumar (VP IR & Treasury) at FMS 2026. Four net-new mechanisms: (1) A NON-OBVIOUS INELASTICITY ARGUMENT — "when supply tightens, CUSTOMERS REDUCE STORAGE HEADROOM BEFORE THEY REDUCE CORE MEMORY CAPACITY, since storage can be added later via pluggable SSD, whereas MEMORY UPGRADES REQUIRE PHYSICAL DISASSEMBLY." Read the derivative: DRAM demand is structurally more defended than NAND demand in a tight market, which is exactly the asymmetry the same week's prints showed ([[SNDK]]'s bit-growth miss vs DRAM spot still rising) and which BofA's own model reproduces (2028 DRAM −8% vs NAND −14%). (2) SCA COVERAGE QUANTIFIED FOR THE FIRST TIME ON THIS PAGE: Strategic Customer Agreements "set to represent ROUGHLY 40% OF VOLUME," with price CEILINGS AND FLOORS in exchange for committed duration — note this is BELOW [[SNDK]]'s >50% FY27 / ~2/3 FY28 bit coverage. (3) MEMORY AT ~50% OF SYSTEM VALUE (from ~10% three decades ago), with the KV-cache mechanism spelled out — "poor KV cache handling forces GPUs to RECOMPUTE PRIOR WORK rather than advance new work, which INFLATES UTILIZATION METRICS WITHOUT PRODUCING REAL OUTPUT" — and the stated tiering: HBM for the hot path, SOCAMM2 main memory for KV overflow, disaggregated DDR5 pools for the long tail. (4) A NEW DEMAND LEG: hyperscalers now "addressing UNDERINVESTMENT ON THE CPU SIDE as workloads shift from human-driven to agent-driven execution," with agentic adoption at a "PRE-SEASON WARM-UP" stage — corroborating the agentic-CPU thesis from [[AMD]]/[[INTC]]/[[ARM]] from the memory side.**
  ⚠️ **THE BULL'S OWN STATED BEAR, LOGGED VERBATIM BECAUSE IT IS THE RIGHT ONE: DB — "we continue to monitor WHETHER MEMORY INTENSITY PER GPU WILL KEEP SCALING WITH CONTEXT-WINDOW GROWTH, OR WHETHER KV-CACHE ORCHESTRATION SOFTWARE CAN BECOME EFFICIENT ENOUGH AT EVICTION AND RECALL THAT SOME OF THIS MEMORY-CONTENT UPSIDE WILL BE ENGINEERED AWAY." Software-defeats-hardware is the cleanest structural risk to the entire memory-content thesis and it now sits on the page in a bull's words.**
  🔴 **SUPPLY-SIDE NET-NEW: Nanya's board approved RAISING 2026 CAPEX TO NT$69.7bn (US$2.2bn) FROM NT$52bn (+34%) to accelerate FAB 5A, a programme ultimately costing UP TO NT$346.6bn (US$10.7bn) BY 2029 — "the biggest investment by a Taiwan DRAM maker in 20 years" (@dnystedt), bringing 10NM-CLASS PROCESSES AND EUV to the fab (@trendforce).** ⚠️ **Two-sided and both sides logged: as SUPPLY it is a fourth credible conventional-DRAM entrant adding leading-edge capacity into 2028+, a real threat to the "no meaningful supply increase" pillar, and the +34% acceleration says Nanya thinks the window is now; as WFE DEMAND it is unambiguously positive for [[ASML]] (new EUV adopter), [[AMAT]], [[KLAC]], [[LRCX]], corroborating what LRCX management told DB at the same conference. ⚠️ RECONCILIATION FLAG: this page already carried Nanya's "US$16bn 2027-29 DRAM capex" from the 07-13 window; the new figure is a company board-approved US$10.7bn THROUGH 2029 over a different period. Do NOT treat them as the same number — flagged for reconciliation, not resolved.**
  **BofA · Simon Woo's Global Memory Tech weekly adds the first sell-side model on this page to put a DATE AND MAGNITUDE on the down-leg: global DRAM industry sales $134bn/$573bn/$847bn/$917bn in 2025/26/27/28, with 4Q26 hikes of DRAM +8% / NAND +3% q/q, stable-to-mildly-correcting 2027 (year-average ASP still ABOVE 2026), then 2028 DRAM −8% / NAND −14% — "no hard-landing, just a healthy correction warranting a high-margin profile." Spot is running better than seasonal: NAND 512Gb–1Tb +5–6%, LEGACY 256Gb +10%+ on Tier-2/white-box rush orders; DRAM spot +1–2% implying +20–40% q/q. Model also embeds [[SKHYNIX]] 2Q DRAM ASP up only 30% q/q "vs 45–70% among peers" with a +25% 3Q recovery, and [[KIOXIA]] guiding 3Q revenue +35% q/q (≈+20% NAND ASP).** ⚠️ **A modelled 2028 DRAM trough of just −8% is the central quantitative claim of the "duration not amplitude" thesis — carry it as the base case to TEST, not as a bear case to fear.**
  **Also: the MU-vs-SKHYNIX-ADR premium exchange (@dylan522p: "corporate governance exists"; @MF_Camillus: the gap is negligible because the ADR itself trades at a substantial premium to the KOSPI line, and index eligibility / accessibility / geopolitical alignment / capital-return prospects matter as much — arguably the premium "should be HIGHER, not lower" given US manufacturing). FinTwit, no numbers — logged because the ADR-vs-KOSPI wedge invalidates naive MU/SKHYNIX multiple comparisons, and because "capital return prospects" is precisely where Samsung and SK Hynix were criticised the same day (Reuters: investors want more cash back "after the pair provided SCANT DETAIL on capital returns when reporting AI-driven record profit"). Barron's separately: "Micron Is Falling as Memory Stocks Take Another Hit" (2026-08-06) — tape only.**

- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the LTA mechanics were disclosed, and they invert how rising contract coverage should be read.** No rating or PT superseded. **The core finding: Samsung said 60-70% of its memory sales will be under LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% q/q against price increases broadly open at 10-20%+, on mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING tenors where a 6th year can be added as the 1st completes. Kioxia separately said it is "on track toward 50% LTA VOLUME COVERAGE FOR CY28 with key customers."** ⚠️ **These are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE, not price locks — so high LTA coverage is bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of the usual late-cycle reading. It also means spot prints are a progressively weaker signal of realised pricing as coverage rises.** **Pricing evidence net-new: Kioxia's Jun-Q ASP +70% q/q LIKE-FOR-LIKE on only low-single-digit bit growth; Samsung DRAM OPM ~80% / NAND OPM ~70% in 2Q with DRAM ASP guided +23% q/q in 3Q and +8% in 4Q (after +91%/+45% in 1Q/2Q); TrendForce July DRAM contract price +low-teens % m/m = 30-50% q/q — but FLAT FOR NAND** (BofA · Woo/Shen/Arya, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **The DRAM/NAND divergence in the July contract print is the detail to carry; the two are not moving together.** ⚠️ **A ceiling on ASP upside also disclosed: Samsung is explicitly trading price for content — ASP guided flat-to-slightly-down through 2027-28 under LTAs and "chipmakers' strategic control (e.g., decent price cut per AI server content increase)" — a share/volume strategy, not price maximisation, with annual OP still rising to W500tn+ on volume and yield.** Duration: **Samsung sees an "ongoing DRAM supply shortage due to AI, EVEN WITH new capacity expansion through 2027-2028"; Kioxia guides CY27 "demand anticipated to exceed supply."** **Spot drivers into August: restocking orders, Tier-2 OEMs resuming Sept/4Q launches despite high cost, and China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming at 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn.** On China supply: **"minimal impact… China-branded memory NOT commonly traded in the spot market."** ⚠️ **Best available confirmation of pricing power, because it shows up in the buyers' own numbers: [[AMZN]] raised FY26 capex ~$200bn → ~$220bn with the increment attributed EXPLICITLY to higher memory costs; [[META]] attributed its raise to component and memory pricing; [[AAPL]] said memory accounts for >100% of its Sept-Q GM compression, with UBS modelling DRAM/NAND ASPs +~30% q/q into December. BofA: hyperscalers are "seemingly NOT BOTHERED by rising prices of chips/memory," with Korean results implying tight supply and elevated pricing "at least through 2027."** Demand mix caveat: **agentic AI and server NAND are carrying the cycle — smartphone NAND demand is FLAT vs CY25 and PC NAND a slight DECLINE on higher BOM costs.** Cross-filed [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[KIOXIA]], [[SNDK]], [[STX]], [[WDC]], [[AAPL]], [[AMZN]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
- **2026-07-28** — **/run-inbox (Micron IR call 07-27 + MS memory webcast 07-28). 2 intra-quarter rows, 1 Current-state block, 1 Risks sub-bullet, Signal-table SCA row rewritten, 2 Sources. No rating, PT, house or BBG estimate superseded.** **The material item is a disclosure that changes how an existing number on this page should be read rather than adding a new one.** Micron IR (Satish) stated that **the ~$100bn RPO across the 14 of 16 SCAs with a pricing framework is calculated as contract volume × the FLOOR price**, and volunteered the caution that **"the pricing is at the ceiling right now… the revenue that we will actually get on the SCAs, we expect will be well above the RPO levels. The RPO is not indicative of the revenue that we will get."** The page previously carried the $100bn as an "ASC606 accounting floor; actual expected materially higher" — that framing was directionally right and is now management-explicit, with the mechanism (floor-price arithmetic against ceiling-price realisation) named. **Second, the SCA architecture is now fully specified and it reconciles two figures the page carried side by side: up to 50%+ of total revenue under SCAs = ~40% priced-framework (band or fixed) + ~10% volume-only take-or-pay priced quarterly + ~50% market**, with the framework tranche "in the low 20%" today against the 25%-of-revenue/16-contracts disclosed at earnings — so management's ">50%" target and Barron's "~40% once fully executed" are the same plan on different tranches, not competing estimates. **All SCAs carry take-or-pay volume.** Ceiling confirmed ≈ the **June-calendar-quarter market price** (already inside the **86% Aug-quarter GM guide**, up from ~85%); floor GM again only *characterised* — "well above" the **fiscal-2018 low-60s%** peak, "we haven't quantified that level" — so **UBS's ~70-75%/$5-per-GB back-solve stays an inference and Bernstein's "meaningful but not infinite" bound stays untested.** **Third, the tightness horizon walked forward a third time** (2026 → 2027 → "beyond calendar 27 and even in 28"; "we actually do not see a line of sight to when the supply demand balance even comes back into balance") — logged as a walk-forward pattern, not a supersession, since the page already carried "no line of sight… through 2028." **Fourth, and the genuinely two-sided item: Satish conceded that customers are cutting the RATE of memory content growth versus prior plans, but attributed it to supply — "not… an optimization that truly reduces the amount of memory you need per unit of hardware."** Folded into the existing SemiAnalysis de-spec Risks bullet rather than logged separately, because the two accounts **agree on the fact and differ only on whether it reverses when supply arrives** — which is now the testable question on the content axis. Also new: **China exposure high-20s% → low-double-digit %** (Chinese-government restrictions on Chinese DC buyers + MU's own NAND-mobile abstention), **~3 nodes ahead of China / 1-gamma EUV >half of DRAM production / 1-delta next / ~65,000 patents**, and **100% of excess cash to be returned over time**. From the MS webcast (Joe Moore + Sean, 07-28): rank order **NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK**; the drawdown reframed as recurring rather than informational (**"you're not positive on Micron at 1200, you're positive at 800"**); the Q3 deceleration accepted outright ("the law of big numbers"); **Sean's three-prong debate resolving by elimination onto the cycle — 3Q y/y peak, 3Q DRAM +15-20%, 4Q needing +60-70% sequential to sustain the second derivative**; TrendForce **DRAM bits 25%→31% this year, ~24% next; NAND high-teens → 20-25% next year** (YMTC third line, 50,000 wafers); inventory up since 2Q **at module makers, not producers**; and corroboration that the **MU buyback waits until year-end on Chips Act restrictions**.
