- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook + Twitter). 2 intra-quarter rows. No MU rating/PT superseded** — nothing published on MU itself; **the entire move was read-through.** **The substantive input is Jefferies research asking “HAS MEMORY PEAKED?” and answering with three numbers: price hikes are tracking 15-20% versus the 25-30% expected, 2027 consensus requires ~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable, and memory prices peak around 1H27** — with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB** and **Rubin push-outs** as near-term headwinds even though the CoWoS buildout stays intact ([[NVDA]] allocation rising to 1.03mn wafers in 2027). **The price-realisation gap (15-20% achieved vs 25-30% expected) is the single most actionable datapoint** — it says the shortage is real but the pricing power assumed in 2027 numbers is not fully materialising, which is a different and more dangerous problem for the memory names than a demand miss would be. **The tape confirmed how MU trades in that world: on the [[SKHYNIX]] Q2 miss, MU fell ~8.9% during the session and a further ~4% after hours, while Hynix itself opened -6% and recovered to -2.3%.** MU is being priced as the high-beta expression of memory-cycle doubt rather than as a share-gain beneficiary of a competitor's HBM4 slip. **MU also fell below $1trn of market cap** (@Hedgeye, 2026-07-27). The Korean session behind it: **KOSPI -10.7% with a sell sidecar and the year's eighth circuit breaker, [[SAMSUNG]] -12%, SK Hynix -13%**, driven by **CXMT's IPO debut at +466% — China's second-largest IPO ever — “making the memory threat feel suddenly very real”** plus a live-Fed-hike scare. - **2026-07-27** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook). 3 intra-quarter rows. No rating, PT or house estimate superseded.** **The run is organised around one question — was Friday's memory selloff right — and the page now carries both sides properly dated.** **The bear side, quantified: Friday saw SNDK -11%, MU/WDC -7%, NBIS -15% and US semis -4.25%, which Rothschild attributes to two narratives only — X chatter about DECLINING NAND SPOT PRICES and TickerTrends flagging an '[[ANTHROPIC]] air pocket'.** On top of that, **CXMT jumped 470-472% on its Shanghai debut to an almost $500bn market cap, making it the most valuable China-listed company** — which matters less as a valuation event than as a funding event for a competitor's capacity. **The bull side is the more rigorous and it is the item worth keeping: FUNDA argues the market is 'making the same mistake twice: reading an efficiency gain in ATTENTION MEMORY as a cut to MEMORY DEMAND.'** Its technical case is specific — **K3 is a 2.8T-parameter MoE activating 16 of 896 experts, with Moonshot itself recommending a high-bandwidth supernode of AT LEAST 64 ACCELERATORS (heavier weights, wider expert parallelism, more scale-up networking)** — and its KV-cache point inverts the bear read: **K3's ~75% compression (vs DeepSeek V4's ~90%) is WHAT MAKES NAND OFFLOAD ECONOMICALLY VIABLE, so it EXPANDS the NAND/DRAM opportunity; 'the bear case has the sign wrong.'** FUNDA has standing on this call — it flagged DeepSeek V4 as the NAND-KV-cache inflection in late April and notes that three months on **'almost everyone is adopting similar approaches, from [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]] to Kimi K3.'** Its enterprise-storage expert call the same week corroborates on demand: **'procurement remains tight, LTAs are doing more of the work, and DRAM/NAND price strength is spreading BEYOND SPOT'** — which, if right, means the spot-price datapoint that drove Friday is the least informative part of the pricing picture. **Two MU-specific items, both net-new: (1) MU and [[META]] published a JOINT WHITE PAPER giving workload-level evidence that LPDDR5X can move beyond mobile into HYPERSCALE SERVERS via the modular SOCAMM2 form factor — MS reads the implication as incremental DRAM content and a richer memory mix per server, as AI infrastructure becomes constrained by power, bandwidth and memory capacity rather than compute. NB this points the OPPOSITE way from the SemiAnalysis 07-24 datapoint already on this page, where LPDDR5X was REMOVED entirely from the AMD MI455X BOM on tight memory supply — the two must be netted, not stacked. (2) The CXMT policy fight is now public with MU as the named opponent: [[AAPL]] is lobbying the administration to allow CXMT/YMTC memory in Apple products sold OUTSIDE the US, and MU is AGGRESSIVELY OPPOSING IT** on domestic-industry and national-security grounds (WSJ). Cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[META]], [[SNDK]], [[WDC]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch). 4 intra-quarter rows. No rating, PT or estimate superseded** (BofA Buy $1,550, TD Cowen Buy $1,600, Mizuho, Susquehanna, MS all stand). **The competitive side of this page changed materially this weekend, and it did so through a bull note on the competitor rather than a bear note on MU. Nomura initiated coverage of ChangXin Memory (CXMT, 688825 CH) at Buy, TP CNY116 — and benchmarked the multiple explicitly off Micron: CXMT should trade at "2x the valuation of its major US competitor, Micron… which has traded at ~10x over the past five years."** The forecast that matters here: **CXMT's global DRAM share going from ~10% today to ~18% by end-2028F on 40-45% bit-CAGR expansion 2026-30F.** That **independently corroborates Susquehanna's 17%-of-DRAM-supply-by-YE28 estimate already on this page (07-25)** — and it does so from a house that is long the stock, which makes the agreement more informative than two bears converging. **CXMT listed in Shanghai on 7/27**, with BofA's APAC desk reporting a **base-case valuation of 15-20x FY27 P/E → ~RMB5T market cap and a bull case of 30x → ~RMB9T** (roughly US$700bn-1.3tn on ~10% DRAM share) — the cleanest illustration on this page of the **cost-of-capital asymmetry MU faces against a state-supported competitor**, and the reason the ASP threat is not simply a function of CXMT's technology. **Both sides of the CXMT question are kept, because they are different arguments and both are true: it is a commodity-DRAM ASP threat, and per BofA/Arya it "remains largely confined to commodity DRAM and is not a credible near-term competitor in HBM3E/HBM4 AI memory."** **Second: Micron is now publicly on the record against an Apple lobbying push — WSJ reports Apple lobbying the White House "aggressively for permission to utilize Chinese memory chips in devices sold outside the US" with MU "fiercely opposed," warning China's makers "could decimate the American memory industry over time just as occurred in steel and manufacturing."** Escalations vs the 06-25 story on file: the ask is now a specific and much more grantable **ex-US carve-out**, and MU's opposition carries an explicit industrial-policy frame. The 06-29 KIS rebuttal still stands as the counter (China restricts foreign supply anyway; CXMT shifted LPDDR→DDR for domestic DCs) — so this changes no supply yet, but it does establish that customer pressure has reached the White House, in the same week as the CXMT initiation and IPO. **Third, the bullish half of Nomura's own work is the strongest demand statement now on this page and it comes from the competitor's analyst: global memory usage rising more than SEVENFOLD over 2026-30F (60%+ bit CAGR) "even after accounting for potential adoption of memory-efficiency technologies (discounting memory usage by 4x)," against all memory makers expanding at only a 30-40% bit CAGR — "that could still be insufficient."** A 4x efficiency haircut still leaves demand outrunning supply by a wide margin; that is the tightest formulation of the structural bull case on file. **Fourth, restated with the consensus gap explicit: BofA/Simon Woo's July checks have Tier-2 OEMs and module makers paying 15-20% higher PC DRAM contract prices m/m, implying +30-40% q/q against TrendForce's +13-18% for 3Q ex-HBM** — and the tape is paying for it, with **MU +8% last week (STX +8%, WDC +9%, SNDK +6%) while the SOX sat -19% off its high.** Also net-new from Arya's defence: **Kimi K3 requires ~1.4TB of HBM per serving instance despite ultra-low API pricing**, and open-weight models "multiply memory demand because every customer deployment creates a new memory endpoint" — the Jevons argument stated as architecture rather than elasticity. ⚠️ One data-integrity note: Nomura's quoted **closing price of CNY8.66 against a CNY116 TP** cannot be reconciled with a 20x-2028F-EPS-of-CNY5.8 derivation; the stock listed 7/27, so the close is pre-listing/stale and only the TP is usable. Cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SNDK]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).

Plainly: **part of current DRAM/NAND pricing rests on an export-control regime the same administration is incentivised to relax.** Cross-filed to [SKHYNIX.md](SKHYNIX.md), [SAMSUNG.md](SAMSUNG.md). AMD Advancing AI 2026" (2026-07-24).** Added, in three places: **(1) HBM technology / share** — the **HBM4 pin-speed bake-off** bullet: AMD MI455X **12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s at 7.6 Gbps** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s at 10.7 Gbps (40% faster)** — a near-tie despite AMD's 50% wider bus, because "Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X," which "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Specifics: **data-center memory prices +25%+ in 3Q** (deceleration vs 2Q's larger increases, "but that has been obvious"); **NAND checks inflecting recently**. **The framing worth carrying into the cycle debate on this page: "LTAs and de-speccing could flatten the amplitude of the cycle vs inflated expectations — but increase duration, which is likely better for stocks in the long run"** — i.e. the SCA/take-or-pay structure is an **amplitude-for-duration trade**, which is the mechanism behind (not a contradiction of) Arete's ~30x-P/E re-rating argument already on the page. **BofA "Global Memory Tech" (Woo/Shen/Arya, 07-23/24)** corroborates on pricing: **PC DRAM July contract prices +15-20% m/m at more Tier-2 OEMs/module makers, implying +30-40% q/q vs consensus +13-18%** (TrendForce, ex-HBM); spot +2-3% w/w; **China memory imports at a record US$32bn (+~250% y/y) despite rising domestic output — "CXMT's expanding capacity has had only a limited impact."** **The bear/tape side, logged as mechanism not just price:** Asian memory was very weak on 07-23 with the **KOSPI -5.7% on no memory-specific newsflow** (Rothschild · Adley, 07-24), possibly a read-across from the US session "or even the Anthropic datapoint" (his unverified TickerTrends read that Anthropic ARR growth has slowed — see [[ANTHROPIC]]); and **@jukan05's 07-23 report that YMTC has surpassed Micron and SanDisk in NAND share (gap to Kioxia now 0.4pp)** triggered selling he then pushed back on: *"**NAND is still in short supply, and that shortage will persist. NVIDIA's CMX racks are currently so constrained by NAND shortages that they cannot even be shipped fully populated.**"* **Both sides stay on the page: YMTC share risk is real; the physical NAND tightness proof point is also real** — and the week's drawdown was driven by share-shift headlines and frontier-lab ARR perception at a moment when the pricing checks were beating. Also logged: **BofA's Arya arguing open-source AI is *bullish* for memory** — closed models amortise demand across shared HBM pools in a few data centres, whereas **open models replicate weights into a separate HBM pool with every self-hosted deployment** (via @jukan05, 07-22); and investors pointing to **CXMT's Monday debut as a supply-risk marker**, against Reuters reporting **CXMT has been repeatedly *raising* prices to Huawei**.
